# R01CAL-A03-DELIVERY-01 verification

## T1 Windows helper qualification

Execution selected on 2026-11-08 (Asia/Singapore) for T1 only against AR-S4.2. Entry HEAD `a88f1bab6687a2234c689b4589b1035d8f4b6618`; inherited modified/untracked documentation is preserved. Ignored run root: `.local/ar-t1-20261108-qualification/`, with `entry-files.json` and `entry-status.txt` capturing the starting worktree. The maintained [acceptance-only harness](../../../tools/qualification/README.md), reader source and CMake wrapper belong in `tools/qualification/`; they are not application integration or dependency-manifest changes.

**AR-Q1 — Reproduced preservation blocker (negative qualification).** In the Windows 13.55 writer, valid existing XMP with tag-like closing text inside CDATA/comments changes unrelated `xmp:Label` values. Adobe's pinned smart handler reads the original and derivative independently. For example, the valid comment case's original value is `before after`; the derivative contains only `before`, despite helper exit 0, no write warning and a complete new provenance record. The CDATA case also replaces the expected trailing text with XML packet text. Both PNG and JPEG reproduce this. Warning rejection and checking only the newly added record therefore cannot meet the preservation contract. Exact fixture/artifact identities and the completed control matrix follow in this entry. T3's positive-qualification dependency is not satisfied; no replacement version, stripping or companion fallback is adopted.

### T1 invocation and retained run basis

From this repository, `R` below denotes the absolute run root `C:/Users/laichungling/Documents/ChatGPT/pdf-to-md-agent-2/.local/ar-t1-20261108-qualification`; `W` is `R/dependencies/exiftool/exiftool-13.55_64/exiftool.exe`; `A` is `R/reader-build/Release/xmp_read.exe`. Configure/build used the existing full-path CMake executable under Visual Studio BuildTools:

```text
cmake.exe -S tools/qualification -B R/reader-build -G "Visual Studio 17 2022" -A x64 -DXMP_SDK_ROOT=R/sdk
cmake.exe --build R/reader-build --config Release --target xmp_read --parallel 2
.venv/Scripts/python.exe tools/qualification/qualify_metadata.py --writer W --reader A --run R/controls-2
```

The table records subsequent exact argument additions to that Python invocation; each uses its own new `--run`. All writer/reader commands have full absolute argument vectors in `<run>/calls/<control>/argv.json`, with complete `stdout.bin`, `stderr.bin` and `process.json`. Preparation uses only an opaque `a19f.png`/`.jpg` copy, never the original. Each helper invocation is a shell-free argument vector with the explicit `-config` first, `-charset filename=UTF8 -charset UTF8`, fixed selectors, then literal absolute input after `--`.

Write suffix: `-overwrite_original -XMP-dc:Title= -XMP-dc:Description= -csv=<private-input.csv>` followed by the fixed four `XMP-pdfmd` property selectors and `XMP-dc:Title-x-default`, `Title-zh-CN`, `Description-x-default`, `Description-zh-CN`, `Source`. No `Compact=OneDesc`/`All`, `-W`, `-w`, output filename template, Directory/FileName assignment, `-htmlDump`, `-ee`, `-stay_open`, stripping or ignore-error option is enabled. Long captions never enter the command line. `controls-3` onward reads `-j -G1 -s -XMP:all -EXIF:all -Warning -Error -- <path>` before and after; earlier `controls-2` used the narrower `XMP-pdfmd:all`/`XMP-dc:all` readback and retains that scope.

The final harness retains the same failing fixtures and writer invocation; added probes expand readback, isolate ICC/raw-JPEG/negative controls, and protect unrelated Dublin Core properties as well as other namespaces. No complete comparison has a harness exception.

Every native process starts after its Python worker joins a Windows Job with a **256 MiB aggregate committed-memory limit, five-second job CPU limit and kill-on-close**; the parent enforces **15 seconds wall time**. stdout/stderr are files with no truncation. Process-only locale removal avoids bundled Perl warnings about the inherited unsupported `C.UTF-8`; it changes no host/global setting. Across complete `controls-2`–`controls-5` and the JSON control, the largest recorded peak job allocation was **85,671,936 bytes**, and the longest recorded native invocation **0.672 seconds**. No time/resource limit fired. These are modest bounded controls, not exploitability estimates, stress maxima or a product process-sandbox implementation.

### T1 observed control matrix

| Control | Observed result and limit |
| --- | --- |
| New record, standard fields, copied/renamed image | CSV controls recover all four app properties, standard title/description language alternatives and source digest; standard JSON decoding recovers complete Chinese strings, ordered physical/printed positions, known/none/unknown states, CR/LF/control/noncharacter distinctions, decomposed text and literal option-like/percent text. The read-only Adobe process receives only the copied image's path, in a directory containing only that image; no writer config, expected record, database, sidecar, source or network is supplied. Full source semantic truth is synthetic, not real conversion acceptance. |
| PNG/JPEG encoded and decoded preservation | Every complete image comparison retains all non-XMP chunk/marker payloads, tables/frame data and IDAT/JPEG scan bytes; same-decoder dimensions/color/alpha/samples also agree. Tests include RGBA, palette/tRNS, 16-bit gray, gamma, useful text, EXIF orientation/artist, PNG iCCP and JPEG ICC/CMYK APP14. Seven canonical repository PDF fixtures are extracted through existing `Evidence` in fresh synthetic stores: RGB/CMYK, SMask, raw CMYK, auto-mask and JPX display cases. Associated original stream records remain in those stores; inherited extraction/color limitations are not recertified as source fidelity. |
| Existing simple and multiple-CDATA XMP | Simple labels and adjacent `first`/`second` CDATA controls preserve values. This does not cancel AR-Q1's concrete counterexamples; no universal multiple-CDATA failure is claimed. |
| Valid tag-like CDATA/comments (AR-Q1) | Both PNG and JPEG silently change `xmp:Label`, while exit 0 and complete independent new-record recovery still occur. The combined multiple-CDATA/tag/comment cases additionally emit an XMP format warning and change values. Valid XML was read successfully before writing by Adobe. These are supported valid-input preservation failures. |
| PNG `caBX` / `eXIf` orders (AR-Q2) | Uses the structural `jumd`/`jumb` control from [upstream #453](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/pull/453), not a fabricated signed C2PA claim. In `caBX`-first order full readback omits Orientation/Artist and warns `IFD0 pointer references previous JUMBFBox directory`; reverse order recovers those EXIF values but warns `JUMBFBox pointer references previous IFD0 directory`. The writer emits no write warning, and raw `eXIf`/`caBX` payloads remain equal. This is a readback/parser defect, not observed chunk deletion or a signature-validity assessment. |
| Extended JPEG | A 297,715-byte UTF-8 RecordJSON plus long descriptive fields produces **23 extension APP1 segments, 1,477,115 extension bytes**. All fragment GUIDs equal the MD5 of the reassembled extension payload; Adobe recovers every expected field from the renamed image alone. Removing extension segments from a separate control still opens with reader exit 0, but RecordJSON and description are absent. Required-field equality detects the omission; reader-open success cannot be the gate. |
| Unterminated CDATA / comment | Both formats return helper exit 0 with warnings indicating XMP parse loss; derivatives can contain the new record. The harness marks these preparations rejected and retains the failed copies. This proves why warnings must be checked independently of exit code; it does not implement application publication rollback. |
| Malformed UTF-8 / resource trace | A small invalid byte sequence in existing XMP reaches the actual full JSON readback: Label becomes six `?` characters without a warning. Adobe rejects the original malformed packet; the helper writes a derivative and the new record still recovers. The actual CLI `EscapeJSON` calls `XMP::FixUTF8` before a caller can assess its returned warnings (packaged script line 3821; source `XMP.pm` lines 2943 onward). The bounded probe exercises that reachable conversion; no broad malformed-input or resource clearance is claimed. |
| EXIF SamplesPerPixel count | PNG/JPEG embed modest count **4096** in a TIFF IFD with dimensions/strip-count fields and no BitsPerSample. XMP-only writes preserve EXIF bytes and full readback returns the count under the Job bounds. The later `ValidateImageData` expansion guard is in `WriteExif.pl` lines 363 onward, called by `WriteExif` during TIFF rewriting. For this fixed allowlist, JPEG `Writer.pl` line 6340's `last unless $$editDirs{IFD0}` and PNG `ProcessPNG_eXIf` line 1399's `EDIT_DIRS{IFD0}` guard avoid that rewrite; normal readback does not invoke this write validation. This is a source-backed exclusion for the fixed XMP-only call, supported by unchanged EXIF payloads, not exclusion by extension. Adding EXIF writes or other APIs reopens it. No enormous count allocation or exploit is attempted. |
| Filename-directed / actual-type controls | Existing OriginalRawFileName is `../outside/source.jpg`; no output-template/rename option is enabled, no metadata-derived output appears at the checked private paths, and only the opaque derivative is overwritten. A copied Chinese `%` name opens with exact byte equality and retains the recorded assigned basename. A TIFF disguised as `.png` is rejected by actual PNG/JPEG structure validation before helper write. These do not certify every destination filesystem or T3's future publication implementation. |
| External metadata stripping | Removing the XMP container from a separate copy leaves no recoverable record. Ordinary copy/rename retains it. This demonstrates the agreed external-transformation limitation, not a successful stripped deliverable. |
| JSON transport (AR-Q3) | The 13.55 JSON importer performs `\\uXXXX` replacement before backslash unescaping (`Import.pm` lines 220–222), so literal escape sequences inside a nested RecordJSON/display spelling are double-decoded. The exact control's writer exits 0 without a warning but the recovered inner JSON is invalid/unequal. CSV quoting only doubles quotes and preserves these sequences; all corresponding CSV checks recover the exact strings. No schema/text weakening, writer patch or version change obtains that transport pass. |

For the comment case the exact valid input property is `<xmp:Label>before<!-- </xmp:Label> --> after</xmp:Label>` inside the generated RDF packet. Adobe reads `before after` before the write and `before` afterwards. The CDATA input is `<xmp:Label><![CDATA[before </xmp:Label> after]]></xmp:Label>`; afterwards its value includes closing packet text instead of the original trailing `after`. Both use only the fixed new-property/descriptive-field allowlist. The [13.58 XMP patch](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/commit/38bdbace3037a89d2c332664fc60bddad118958a) supplies source support for that class of closing-tag handling failure; it was inspected, not applied. The [13.61 patch](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/commit/9b8664dfcf2c609d3cc8925d380e98ad2a379f9b) supplies the later UTF-8/count-path comparison; no newer helper was executed or adopted.

### T1 disposition, preservation and limits

**Completed negative bounded qualification: ExifTool 13.55 is not qualified for the adopted supported workflow.** AR-Q1 is a reproduced valid-input preservation blocker regardless of the narrower passing controls; AR-Q2 confirms a distinct readback defect. AR-Q3 excludes the tested JSON transport while leaving documented CSV available for later candidate qualification. These results require the [concrete dependency decision](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#t1-qualification-and-dependency-decision); they do not authorize a silent development upgrade, backport, replacement writer, valid-image exclusion, metadata stripping or sidecar.

T1's investigation contribution can close with this result; **T3 remains blocked on a positive qualification for an explicitly selected candidate/invocation**, as well as T2's separate dependency. T2 remains independent and unselected. T4/T5 are not started. No whole-batch AR-8/AR-12, application save/reopen/export/atomicity, Windows 11/Linux/macOS/browser, installed-product or source-content acceptance is claimed. The modest resource checks leave broader malformed-input behavior unknown; there is no need to resolve every such unknown to establish the already decisive negative result. No original A03 task/version/package, source PDF, credential, application source or lockfile is mutated by qualification.

Original and failed useful work remains: downloaded archives, SDK sources, static/dynamic build logs, earlier harness snapshots, all original/prepared/copied/stripped/missing-extension fixture bytes, expected fields, transport files, synthetic stores and full process outputs below the run root. `artifact-identities.json` records acquisitions/builds/tool hashes, with complete local runtime-member and result manifests retained there. These ignored raw artifacts are available on this host, not implicitly published to external reviewers. The maintained harness, exact failure construction, essential hashes, invocation and semantic observations above make the dependency assessment understandable without them.

### T1 documentation impact and checks

This entry owns observations, AR-Q1–AR-Q3, exact identity and the bounded verdict. Tickets own T1 completion and the unsatisfied downstream gate; discussion owns the concrete unadopted dependency alternatives and their tradeoffs. The design keeps AR-S4.2's requirements and adds routes from acquired-artifact/coverage claims to actual qualification, without adopting a writer change. The spec replaces obsolete tickets-only/no-acquisition continuation with the current authority/evidence links and leaves all repaired-runtime acceptance open. WORK_ITEMS records the reached dependency-choice trigger and preserves offline delivery priority; it does not copy the verdict. CURRENT_STATE points to the dependency decision and T1 stop. INDEX and PROJECT_LAYOUT locate the new maintained acceptance harness/usage guide. No second execution ledger is added.

Consumer review covered README/setup, AGENTS, the document/image design owners, DOCUMENT_ENGINE, the phase conditions, active spec/design/tickets/discussion routes and original A01/A02/A03 evidence. The app's existing hash-path, image-only save/preview and export descriptions remain accurate because no application behavior changed. PHASE_PLAN's open Stage B/D acceptance and README's installed-product limits remain valid; no copied ticket status or phase closure is added. Historical source/audit/planning reviews retain their dates, input identities and unexecuted-at-that-stage wording. T1 does not change those historical observations into runtime acceptance.

Checks: the final Python harness compiles; the actual dynamic SDK reader build and the source-led qualification controls above executed. `python -X utf8 -B tools/docs/check_docs.py` passes with 136 Markdown files, 6,488 checked local destinations, 503 bundled source files and zero errors. `git diff --check` passes with inherited CRLF normalization notices; a separate scan includes the changed/untracked Markdown and harness files and finds no trailing whitespace. Main-agent review checks the distinction between new-record recovery and unrelated metadata preservation, raw-chunk equality and reader omission, helper exit status and diagnostics, small bounded resource observations and general safety, and completed negative assessment versus T3's positive gate. No subagent or independent human review is claimed.

Entry-relative hashing identifies nine changed documentation files and four new maintained harness/usage files; **the other 854 of 863 entry files remain byte-identical, with none missing**. This includes application code/tests, all manifests/lockfiles, original A01/A02/A03 owners, inherited unrelated document edits and all six historical specification captures. `closeout-preservation.json` retains the comparison and hashes. HEAD remains `a88f1bab6687a2234c689b4589b1035d8f4b6618`; changes are local/uncommitted, with no push, PR or publication. Broad application/desktop regression tests are not rerun for this acceptance-only harness and dependency assessment; application integration was explicitly outside T1.

### ExifTool later-fix applicability

**AR-D3 — Production status does not clear the selected writer.** The official [release history at 13.61](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/blob/13.61/Changes) still designates 13.55 as production. Inspected the official release changes through 13.61 and the relevant 13.55 implementation paths. Findings distinguish fixes already present, optional features excluded by the planned invocation, relevant fidelity corrections, and resource concerns whose exact reachability still needs qualification. No exploit, metadata rewrite or runtime reproduction was executed.

| Fix / primary evidence | Applicability to the specified helper |
| --- | --- |
| [CVE-2026-7580 CNA record](https://cveawg.mitre.org/api/cve/CVE-2026-7580), fixed in 13.54 by [GM metadata patch](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/commit/5a8b6b6ead12b39e3f32f978a4efd0233facbb01) | Code injection through crafted GM metadata with `-ee`, affecting versions through 13.53. **Already fixed before 13.55**; no `-ee` in the selected helper. It is not an unfixed post-13.55 issue merely because an advisory is later. |
| [CVE-2026-3102 CNA record](https://cveawg.mitre.org/api/cve/CVE-2026-3102), fixed in 13.50 by [MacOS patch](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/commit/e9609a9bcc0d32bd252a709a562fb822d6dd86f7) | Command injection in macOS `SetMacOSTags`, affecting versions through 13.49. **Already fixed before 13.55**; the planned writer is the Windows distribution and does not write OS tags. The cross-platform acceptance reader is Adobe, not this writer. |
| [13.59 commit](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/commit/2200871d9cef988051d2a99d67df3bda6cbb30a8): `FilenameSPrintf` strips directories from `%o` | `%o` in `-W` output naming can draw from source `OriginalRawFileName` / `OriginalFileName`. Excluding `-W` and metadata-controlled output paths removes this specific feature path. The release says “Security update” without explicitly mapping that label to this one patch; the security interpretation of this change is an inference from the code, not a complete upstream advisory. Shell-free execution alone would not prevent ExifTool's own template expansion. |
| [13.58 XMP changes](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/commit/38bdbace3037a89d2c332664fc60bddad118958a), then 13.59 write-error changes | **Relevant to preservation.** 13.58 corrects multiple-CDATA substring extraction and closing-tag matching around CDATA/comments. 13.59 promotes several missing-terminator/closing-tag write warnings to errors. App-generated JSON/XML escaping avoids needing CDATA in new records, but valid pre-existing XMP can contain it. Warning rejection helps fail safely; it does not repair valid metadata parsing or prove 13.55 reports every case. These are fidelity/error-handling findings, not an asserted new RCE. |
| [PNG EXIF/C2PA issue and maintainer confirmation](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/pull/453), included in [13.60](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/commit/980409314fddbd83d9092b5c160fafab7f67fd52) | **Relevant to supported PNG metadata.** Shared processed-directory tracking across embedded `caBX`/`eXIf` chunks can report a previous-directory error and omit EXIF. The patch resets tracking per chunk. Both chunk orders belong in preservation acceptance; freshly generated PNGs usually lacking these chunks do not exclude extracted or pre-existing images. This is a preservation bug, not an assigned injection CVE. |
| 13.60 `-htmlDump` HTML injection and `-stay_open` closed-input hang | These optional output/process modes are explicitly unused. ExifTool HTML is not the app's Markdown preview, and the helper is not a persistent stdin worker. This excludes those feature paths without claiming all parser bugs are excluded. |
| [13.61 hardening](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/commit/9b8664dfcf2c609d3cc8925d380e98ad2a379f9b): `FixUTF8`, EXIF `SamplesPerPixel` guard and other parser changes | **Conditional resource relevance remains.** 13.55 CLI `EscapeJSON` calls `XMP::FixUTF8`; malformed existing text can reach readback conversion before a caller checks warnings. The new `WriteExif::ValidateImageData` count guard concerns EXIF/TIFF structures, which can be embedded in JPEG/PNG. Outer file extension alone does not prove these paths unreachable. The release notes report corrupt-metadata memory hardening, but this source audit does not establish exploitability, exact impact or successful mitigation in our final invocation. Warning rejection after the helper returns cannot bound earlier resource use. |

The 13.56 XML change was reverted in 13.57; selecting an intermediate release by number alone would not resolve the XMP findings. Other fixes cover FLIF, QuickTime, PGF, geolocation databases and camera-specific formats/features outside the selected display-format workflow. Nested metadata still needs exact-path reasoning; a `.jpg` or `.png` suffix is not a parser isolation boundary. The two identified CVEs above are not a claim that all historical or future advisories have been exhaustively excluded.

The [13.55 CLI](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/blob/13.55/exiftool), [XMP parser](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/blob/13.55/lib/Image/ExifTool/XMP.pm), [EXIF writer](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/blob/13.55/lib/Image/ExifTool/WriteExif.pl) and [PNG handler](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/blob/13.55/lib/Image/ExifTool/PNG.pm) were read to relate changes to the proposed invocation rather than treating release-note keywords as proof. Scope excludes broad resource isolation redesign. The spec instead makes the exact helper-call qualification a prerequisite to adopting this candidate and extends AR-12's discriminating controls without reducing supported-image fidelity.

**Assessment and recommendation:** later changes cannot all be dismissed for our use. Retain the user's production-first preference and 13.55 as a conditional candidate, but do not label it security-cleared or compatibility-proven. Establish whether the supported workflow reaches the concrete defective paths before integration. If an applicable defect prevents the preserved contract, prefer a production release carrying the fix; if none exists, present a concrete alternative or narrowly justified development-release exception rather than silently choosing one. This assessment completes the requested spec audit; runtime qualification remains unexecuted.

### Comparative preservation and negative-control observations

**AR-Q1:** 13.61 preserves the exact valid `before </xmp:Label> after` CDATA value and Adobe's `before after` interpretation of the comment example in both formats, without write/readback warnings. The 13.55 arm still corrupts/truncates them. Simple, multiple-CDATA and combined valid XMP structures also pass in the full 13.61 run. This establishes the tested correction; it does not erase the original 13.55 failure or prove every possible XMP structure.

**AR-Q2:** both 13.61 PNG chunk orders return `IFD0:Orientation = Rotate 90 CW`, `Artist = Artist` and `OriginalRawFileName = ../outside/source.jpg`, with neither directory-collision warning. `caBX`/`eXIf` payloads remain exact. The 13.55 `caBX`-first arm omits those EXIF values and warns; reverse order retains EXIF but warns about JUMBF. Thus actual useful values, diagnostics and raw chunk bytes are checked separately. The fixture is the upstream structural JUMBF control, not a signed-C2PA validation.

**Full valid-input matrix:** every new record and expected standard field recovers exactly through writer readback and the independent copied-image reader. Unrelated XMP leaves, including `dc:creator`/`dc:subject`, remain present. Non-XMP container payloads and encoded picture data compare exactly; decoded dimensions/color/alpha/samples also agree with the same PyMuPDF 2.0 decoder. Cases include PNG 16-bit/palette/transparency/gamma/iCCP, JPEG RGB/CMYK/ICC/APP14, EXIF orientation, all seven existing PDF display extractions and raw JPEG controls. Source-language text, CR/LF/control/noncharacter spellings, decomposed Unicode and literal escapes recover through the selected CSV transport. No source-image extraction limitation is promoted to general source fidelity.

**Extended/standalone controls:** the 297,715-byte RecordJSON produces 23 JPEG extension segments and 1,477,115 extension bytes. Every fragment GUID matches the reconstructed payload MD5; all expected fields recover from the renamed Chinese/percent-name image alone, without expected-record files, writer configuration, source/store, sidecar or network supplied to the reader. The separate missing-extension copy opens with Adobe exit 0 while omitting RecordJSON; the expected-field check detects it. The stripped copy loses the record as expected. The type-mismatched TIFF is rejected before native writing. Metadata-derived original filenames do not direct output outside the preparation paths.

**Malformed/count controls:** 13.61 rejects all four unterminated CDATA/comment writes with errors and leaves each preparation copy unchanged; 13.55's earlier successful-exit/warning behavior remains historical evidence. The modest embedded EXIF count 4096 returns intact under the bounds. Source inspection confirms that the intended XMP-only invocation does not rewrite those TIFF IFDs: packaged 13.61 `Writer.pl:6376` and `PNG.pm:1400` guard on `EDIT_DIRS{IFD0}`; its `WriteExif.pl:375` contains the later count guard. This is an exact-path exclusion of that write expansion for the fixed allowlist, not exclusion by suffix or a stress test. Full JSON readback still reaches `XMP::FixUTF8` via packaged `exiftool.pl:3826`; the small malformed probe exercises conversion without claiming universal resource safety.

**AR-Q3 remains:** JSON import still double-decodes the literal escapes and yields invalid/unequal nested RecordJSON in both candidates. These expected failures validate the negative control and leave CSV selected. No source string, schema, useful metadata or expectation is stripped or weakened to pass.

**AR-Q4 — Remaining malformed-XMP acceptance evidence gap.** In both PNG/JPEG UTF-8 controls the pre-existing Label contains the invalid bytes `c0 80 f5 80 80 80`. The 13.61 write succeeds without a diagnostic and preserves those raw invalid bytes in the derivative; JSON readback prints six `?` characters. Adobe opens with exit 0 but omits Label, while the new record recovers. This is not a newly reproduced valid-input preservation loss: the input value is already malformed and its raw bytes remain exact. It does show that the tested write/readback plus reader-open checks do not establish a malformed-XMP rejection boundary. No strict validity/pre-publication rejection check is implemented or qualified here. Passing the valid matrix must not silently turn this control into evidence of malformed-input rejection.

**Correction of an earlier interpretation:** the original T1 narrative said Adobe rejected the malformed UTF-8 packet. Its preserved raw `controls-3/calls/png-malformed-utf8-before/process.json` actually reports exit 0 and `stdout.bin` is `[]`; the new run confirms that tolerant-open/property-omission behavior. Unterminated CDATA also opens and exposes an incomplete value. This correction preserves the original raw evidence and valid-input negative assessment; it changes only the unsupported interpretation that reader-open/exit already supplied strict malformed-packet validation.

### Comparative disposition and next decision

**Disposition: inconclusive for complete qualification of the intended preparation boundary; positive for the bounded valid-input 13.55/13.61 comparison.** ExifTool 13.61 clears the reproduced AR-Q1/AR-Q2 controls and every tested valid-input case, while 13.55 remains not qualified. AR-Q3 is a reproduced rejected transport in both. AR-Q4 identifies the precise missing evidence against the design's malformed-input gate: a selected and verified acceptance/rejection condition that recognizes the retained invalid-XMP case before publication, rather than trusting successful write/readback or Adobe-open status. The present exact invocation does not supply that evidence. This is not a demand to prove safety for every malformed input, a claim of new valid-image loss or permission to reject all valid pre-existing XMP.

Recommended next decision: retain CSV and consider a separately bounded qualification of explicit malformed-XMP validation/rejection with this exact candidate, preserving valid metadata and the existing contract, before deciding whether to adopt the 13.61 development-release exception. The observed valid-input improvements provide no reason to start a replacement writer now. No guard, product exception, dependency/lockfile change or integration is adopted by this execution. T3 remains unavailable under its existing qualification, product-selection and T2 dependencies; T2–T5 are not executed. ### AR-Q4-C1: reachable nested bytes exceed the bounded outer locator

**Finding/impact:** an outer iTXt/APP1 locator cannot establish the complete original-XMP inventory for the selected full-readback call. Reachability is not excluded by the XMP-only write allowlist, `.png`/`.jpg`, successful reading or Adobe's returned properties. A precheck covering only those outer packets would leave packets the helper actually processes unchecked. This is a coverage gap, not a new valid-input writer-loss finding or a tested failure of an implemented strict guard.

Pinned source establishes distinct write and read responsibilities:

- `PNG.pm:1360–1406` sends read-side `eXIf`/compressed-EXIF contents to `ProcessTIFF`; only its write branch tests `EDIT_DIRS{IFD0}`. JPEG's write-side `Writer.pl:6376` likewise skips an EXIF rewrite for this allowlist, while read dispatch in `JPEG.pm:45–58` still enters EXIF. `Exif.pm:1477–1490` routes TIFF tag `0x2bc` to XMP. Thus the earlier count-expansion **write** exclusion remains correct but does not exclude **reading nested XMP**.
- `JPEG.pm:293–298` dispatches Photoshop APP13, and `Photoshop.pm:263–269` routes resource `0x0424` to XMP. TIFF tag `0x8649` also enters Photoshop; TIFF JUMBF, PNG `caBX` and JPEG APP11 reach box/container dispatch. `PNG.pm:680–760`, `934–1045` and `1157–1268` additionally cover compressed textual data and legacy hex/raw profiles containing XMP, EXIF or Photoshop. They cannot all be declared absent because outer iTXt is absent. No complete raw traversal/decompression implementation for these paths is qualified here.
- The gap also occurs **inside an outer XMP document**: `XMP.pm:201,925` maps `http://ns.nikon.com/asteroid/1.0/` to `Nikon::ast`; `Nikon.pm:13325–13345` defines `ast:XMLPackets` as base64-encoded nested XMP. Read-side `XMP.pm:3814–3835` decodes that property and calls `ProcessDirectory` again, with `NoBlockSave=1`; the branch is conditioned on `not IsWriting`, not on `-ee`. Namespace/table-directed decoding and recursive packet provenance are therefore required beyond checking the containing XML serialization. The source's default strict-MWG/Validate exclusions are not enabled by the selected invocation.

`R/source-trace.json` retains exact source hashes and bounded excerpts. Adobe's PNG handler reads the standard XMP chunk, and its JPEG handler separately caches/reconciles supported Exif/Photoshop/standard/extended metadata; neither supplies an inventory of every packet reached by ExifTool. No field returned by either tolerant reader is used as a raw-byte validity authority.

Eight tiny generated witnesses place the same packet in PNG `eXIf` tag `0x2bc`, JPEG EXIF tag `0x2bc`, JPEG Photoshop resource `0x0424`, or PNG outer `ast:XMLPackets`. Each has a well-formed constructed counterpart and a counterpart containing the exact AR-Q4 invalid bytes `c0 80 f5 80 80 80` in the **inner** Label. They are reachability probes, not a claim of full XMP/RDF compatibility or preservation acceptance. The shared original logical packet hashes are `6075ac4bccb2516e84dd2d581c55a0d48b25e8149adb81a1046385dd03b12034` (256-byte counterpart) and `0ecef772a74fa33618f15adb4fb45419680af27afaffb81fe37567cc074d379d` (241-byte invalid counterpart). Original byte spans below are zero-based, end-exclusive; the Nikon spans contain the original **base64 spelling**, separately associated with those logical packet bytes.

| Witness | File SHA-256 | Original source span |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `png-exif-valid` | `a6d3d775903179bf3db3992f0afa6bf013b685f53eb0f0266c0dfc9ce65223a1` | `[67,323)` |
| `jpeg-exif-valid` | `fa1a6add1e6807401888dfe0f66e2d93fd4a36a77c972bf396a744180fa8dcb1` | `[38,294)` |
| `jpeg-photoshop-valid` | `d88c5cbf9b5b82104e87e1760068de7600f97890b9c83031f853e35838040a9d` | `[32,288)` |
| `png-encoded-nested-valid` | `75afccce0e4cf4dcf15ba8f30e6b5b25694a69260437c2a0b6582e4fe2b7bc95` | `[297,641)` |
| `png-exif-invalid-utf8` | `74436bb5703b08d169b7b8227bdeef9590d4b0a908860835836b12a17266a7b9` | `[67,308)` |
| `jpeg-exif-invalid-utf8` | `ecd4d50da7061a3ed18b910c023924406e4d69c45aa0002a622e58e2b76e0a25` | `[38,279)` |
| `jpeg-photoshop-invalid-utf8` | `4b960ae8fa977efe24c66bce406ef0b6d8cb94239bfe34dccc25646edb49038e` | `[32,273)` |
| `png-encoded-nested-invalid-utf8` | `bfc006f9dc8ee15dfea342c00b6cec3a22bdd4f293aa9680ae41472ff62d534d` | `[297,621)` |

All eight exact full-readbacks exit 0 with empty stderr: each counterpart returns `XMP-xmp:Label = Nested exact original`, while each invalid counterpart returns six `?` characters. The PNG EXIF Adobe reads exit 1 with `No XMP recovered`; JPEG EXIF/Photoshop reads exit 0 with `[]`; both Nikon reads exit 0 returning the **outer base64 property** without the nested Label. These differences establish why reader output cannot certify coverage. The `-b -XMP` TIFF control does recover the exact nested packet; this useful raw access is preserved, not dismissed. But the Nikon raw-block control returns only the outer document, matching the `NoBlockSave` source path. One successful block extraction is not evidence of a complete packet inventory across other carriers/namespace dispatches.

The probe also inventories verified copies of **all 38 prior full-suite original inputs**, including the exact earlier malformed PNG/JPEG cases, together with these eight witnesses. It retains 19 directly located outer packet spans/hashes and all inspected carrier spans/hashes. Forty inputs explicitly report unavailable coverage, and six report outer inventory only without reachability certification; none is accepted or silently labeled globally XMP-absent. JPEG scan suffixes, ExtendedXMP association/assembly, PNG legacy/compressed/nested carriers and trailing data remain explicitly opaque/unqualified where applicable. These are limits of this exploratory probe, not malformed-input verdicts, valid-input support reductions or replacement acceptance criteria. Generator-known inner spans are provenance for these fixtures only; they are not passed off as an arbitrary-input extractor.

### AR-Q4 bounded disposition and remaining evidence

**Disposition: inconclusive, stopped at stage one under AR-Q4-C1.** The missing evidence is a complete original-byte inventory/extraction boundary covering all XMP packets reached by the exact write/full-readback path, including schema-directed nested serializations. Covering those paths would require container/TIFF/resource/profile traversal plus namespace-specific binary decoding and recursive coverage bookkeeping, beyond the agreed small outer locator. This run does not substitute a general metadata parser, infer source-backed exclusions that the controls disprove, or reject supported valid metadata wholesale. Even the Nikon witness alone prevents an outer-serialization syntax check from closing this gap. The investigation does not prove that no narrower future solution exists; its selection/coverage proof remains open.

Stages two through four are **not run**: no strict input-rejection/zero-write guard is qualified, no genuine-no-XMP creation is tested, and no CDATA/comment/namespace/DTD/external-resolution compatibility, ExtendedXMP reconstruction, postcheck corruption, bound-firing or guarded complete-valid-suite result is claimed. ### Source audit and AR-Q4-C1-R1

The source audit follows **dispatch and buffer meaning**, rather than treating loaded modules or names as closure. `R/source-excerpts.json` retains hashed, line-numbered excerpts; `source-closure.json` records conditions and remaining obligations. The inspected core routes are:

| Route and exact source locations | Conditions / original versus derived bytes |
| --- | --- |
| `PNG.pm:908–1035,1157–1407,1412–1688` | Raw chunk `dbuf` enters table-selected `FoundPNG`/`ProcessDirectory`. iTXt/tEXt/zTXt and legacy raw profiles can create decompressed/hex-decoded buffers before downstream processing. eXIf/zXIf reach TIFF on reads independently of the writer's edit-directory gates. Writer text relocation and its returns differ from read traversal; neither excludes nested read packets. `caBX` selects `Jpeg2000::Main`; meTa selects generic XML. |
| `ExifTool.pm:7600–7660,7770–7884,8339–8415`; `JPEG.pm:45–58,277–298,327–368` | JPEG reads TIFF, Photoshop, standard/extended XMP, APP11 JUMBF and trailers. Multipart assembly and offset-keyed extension storage precede callbacks, so original segments/duplicates need separate capture. Default GUID selection and successful assembly are not digest/full-coverage proofs. Scan/trailer exits cannot certify global absence. |
| `Exif.pm:1478–1492,1997–2016,4465–4483,6917–7100`; `Photoshop.pm:255–272`; `ExifTool.pm:9098–9145,9385–9484` | TIFF XMP `0x2bc`, Photoshop `0x8649`, JUMBF and IRB XMP `0x0424` reach indirect dispatch. `PROCESS_PROC`, explicit `ProcessProc`, conditional tables, `RawConv` and duplicate-directory guards matter. A directory's `DataPt` can remain the parent buffer while `DirStart`/`DirLen` identify the child; conversion can replace it before dispatch. |
| `XMP.pm:3062–3099,3690–3760,3814–3838,4346–4408,4550–4680`; `Nikon.pm:13322–13348` | Double encoding / UTF conversion and character-data/entity/value decoding can precede a later capture. The read-only namespace-directed branch can base64-decode and recurse; native base64 truncation must not supply lossless-original evidence. Opaque properties, generic XML and same-document namespace ranges remain distinct from new serializations. |
| `Writer.pl:6364–6386,6452–6548`; `WriteXMP.pl:779–832` | Fixed XMP editing has edit-directory skips and assembles JPEG standard/selected extension data before writer capture. `XMP_CAPTURE` calls `ProcessXMP` for existing properties; its distinct capture semantics do not prove read/write equivalence. These branches were read as source, never invoked. |

**AR-Q4-C1-R1 — recognized Brotli subrange returns before decoding.** PNG `PNG.pm:343–346` routes `caBX` into the box parser. JPEG `ExifTool.pm:8358–8413` reconstructs APP11 JUMBF and enters the same parser. `Jpeg2000.pm:484–510` recognizes a `brob` value beginning `xml ` / `Exif` / `jumb`, selecting `ProcessBrotli`. `ProcessJpeg2000Box:1223–1253,1320–1337` passes the original **whole enclosing `DataPt`**, with child `DirStart`/`DirLen`. `ProcessJUMB:777–797` retains that buffer. But `ProcessBrotli:1392–1419` ignores those offsets: it reads `substr($$dataPt,0,4)`, classifies that as the type and returns 1 with `Unknown Brotli box type` when it is the enclosing box length. The native `unbro` call and downstream `ProcessXMP` are never reached for this buffered carrier.

This is a pre-callback missing route, rather than a reason to call the carrier absent. Capturing an entry/return or the encoded span could retain evidence of the unresolved carrier, but cannot by itself supply its decoded packet/recursive child inventory. No source-closed supplemental route was established within the unchanged native traversal. Supplying a different buffer to repair this branch would change dispatch; independently decoding/traversing its contents requires its own justified source/consumption/compatibility correspondence. Neither is silently substituted for observation-only instrumentation here.

The existing bundled `IO::Uncompress::Brotli` reports 0.004001. Its PM hash is `e5236b28d5dee30e99fee9d4cdd4379ed34c91872ca388aef7b33704abc8c24a`, native XS DLL `7a2d367a0ddea0c422647b85f51a43482679826d64b7616212d06bf3d3ef2b1e`. Its documented one-shot/streaming API returns decoded output without a consumed-input/final-completion result. The [version-matched publisher XS](https://fastapi.metacpan.org/v1/source/MGV/IO-Compress-Brotli-0.004001/Brotli.xs), SHA-256 `1cca32f51b1b44873a9d99c1c3543a3bc66c9e5a2187122e17bc010d6daacfef`, likewise returns output from `unbro:26–41` and discards remaining-input/status at `decompress:60–88`. This is API/source guidance, **not proof that the frozen DLL was built from that exact XS**, nor a qualified complete-consumption mapping. Ordinary Perl-side dumping or a generated encode/decode roundtrip does not close that obligation.

The shared table documents `brob` principally for JXL. These small PNG/JPEG witnesses establish actual source recognition and the return condition, **not normative acceptance of every JXL-style box inside JUMBF**. Compatibility/source-backed exclusion remains unresolved; the study does not exclude the class or label it malformed to obtain a pass. This distinction is why the result is inconclusive rather than a newly qualified/rejected writer. Further vendor/trailer/transitive closure was not completed after this decisive source-route/compatibility gap, and the route table is not represented as exhaustive.

### Bounded read-only witnesses and exact retained artifacts

New maintained [stage-one reproduction tool](../../../tools/qualification/study_xmp_source_closure.py), SHA-256 `9c755f2b314d246621b0d3a8e39fb256cfcc1ed5973fe5e1b7c5f456ef550d47`, builds four tiny constructor-known witnesses. Each image contains one constructed `jumb` box, a 17-byte XML-type `jumd` description and either UUID-XMP raw bytes or a `brob` `xml ` payload. Both use the earlier exact 256-byte logical packet SHA-256 `6075ac4bccb2516e84dd2d581c55a0d48b25e8149adb81a1046385dd03b12034`. The copied native raw **codec**, not an ExifTool metadata writer, compresses that fixed packet to 157 bytes, SHA-256 `ec30323ee6abbd66b5abb45300dce1d0406301841a9eefa1a1d62dd9b8acaee3`. A separate raw decode with a fixed 1,024-byte output bound equals the original. This verifies the generated fixture edge only; it does not prove full consumption for arbitrary compressed carriers or source closure.

| `png-jumb-uuid-raw.png` | `ce661c7d292d391cd1a2261fb412e0c228db1ca0f8435020ee31e4c9305cf64b` | raw `[98,354)` |
| `jpeg-jumb-uuid-raw.jpg` | `eac2f8ce1adf76665c23c72f5eaa5cf3ec504b8707e4a1d2ba216e61e17f465a` | raw `[71,327)` |
| `png-jumb-brob.png` | `0aeed39cbe3d40bfe3ce80f64f9c1d3a66cd8aeddc9a7c178911f8087175c713` | Brotli `[86,243)` |

The outer JUMBF starts at PNG offset 41 / JPEG offset 14. Its child payload starts 41 bytes later; `xml ` is at that payload start and the compressed span follows four bytes later. Thus the actual whole-buffer prefix is enclosing length `00 00 00 ca`, while the recognized child type is `78 6d 6c 20`. Generator-known carrier spans, parent/child and occurrence identities, encoded/original/logical hashes and transform basis are preserved in `packet-source-transform-occurrences.json` and `closure-witness/expected-inventories.json`. These are **expected fixture inventories**, never asserted to be a completed native extraction manifest. Equal packet hashes do not deduplicate occurrences.

The **entire** child—including construction, two raw codec calls, eight native image reads, hashing and inventories—shares one pre-start **256 MiB aggregate Job memory / five-second Job CPU / 15-second parent wall** bound; there is no per-fragment reset. Observed peak Job memory is **67,481,600 bytes**, worker duration **2.828 seconds**, exit 0; no bound fires. Actual aggregate CPU time is not separately reported by the existing harness. These are tiny route controls, not resource/stress acceptance.

Four supplemental source-route reads use the same prefix with `-v3 -- ABSOLUTE_IMAGE`. All eight exit 0 with empty stderr. Both compressed controls return `[minor] Unknown Brotli box type`; verbose output shows recognition as `BrotliXMP`, then the wrong enclosing-length type and early return. ### AR-T2-01 — large-image data-URL navigation blocks

Later scoped execution is assessed at [the AR-S4.3 protocol result](#final-t2-protocol-evidence-and-scoped-assessment). The following finding retains its original data-URL code/input basis.

**Supported finding: P2, unresolved T2 acceptance blocker.** Large-link fixture `383c9f0035e04e99956e02bb85ef5828` contains the full registered 3,004,903-byte PNG (SHA-256 `700c083b0861ab1dd9a952a154f66c815277a04a2edb0bb63a872238312744c6`), 1200 by 1300 pixels. Four repeated embeds decode successfully from those complete bytes. Its operative hyperlink contains the complete 4,006,562-character data URL. Clicking it in the normal isolated desktop navigates to `about:blank#blocked`, rather than displaying that image. Return restores its matching document. The passing test explicitly records `unresolved: true`; it does not count the negative control as successful image-link delivery.

The 355,256-byte, 417 by 452 small control opens through its 473,698-character data URL after the parent CSP correction. The earlier parent policy omitted data-frame navigation and produced `ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CSP` for that small control; permitting app-bound data frames and restricting native navigation to PNG/JPEG data fixes that omission without allowing source data/file URLs. The 3 MB control continues to block after that correction, before a frame-navigation event reaches the instrumented observer. Complete data remains present; no image quota, truncation or resampling workaround is introduced.

Chromium's [URL Mojo definition](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/HEAD/url/mojom/url.mojom) declares a 2,097,152-character URL IPC bound, and the [official source constant](https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/url/+/e31794f9c3f0da803693b89f6da38210000a7de8/url_constants.h) describes its matching inter-process limit. This is consistent with the observed size-dependent failure; the exact compiled rejection branch was not instrumented. The version-tagged Chromium URL source was unavailable through the documentation tool, so that source lookup is not represented as a complete binary-to-source trace. [Electron 45.0.0's DEPS](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/electron/electron/v45.0.0/DEPS) agrees with the observed Chromium version. Actual failure, rather than an assumed maximum acceptable image size, establishes the blocker.

**Required next decision:** revisit the image-link transport under the [open method discussion](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#t2-desktop-mechanism-blocker-and-next-decision), preserving complete selected bytes, sandbox isolation, identity guards and Return. No Blob/protocol alternative is adopted or implemented in this ticket. T2 remains incomplete and its T3 prerequisite is unsatisfied. This does not reopen naming/provenance contracts or resume writer qualification.

## AR-S4.3 T2 protocol implementation and verification

The subsequent 2026-11-09 user instruction authorizes T2 implementation, scoped fixes, tests, isolated desktop verification and records only, preserving full bytes, sandbox isolation, selected-version identity and Return to document, then stop. The final scoped assessment is below. Electron main owns preparation/activation and session/frame-bound serving; the renderer coordinates load generations and cached Return. The checks below assess the selected contribution. Original AR-T2-01 observations retain their original basis.

### Protocol implementation and retained intermediate checks

Applicable official Electron 45.0.0 guidance was retrieved before implementation: [protocol lifecycle and Response handler](https://github.com/electron/electron/blob/v45.0.0/docs/api/protocol.md), [request/frame context and header hooks](https://github.com/electron/electron/blob/v45.0.0/docs/api/web-request.md), [native frame navigation](https://github.com/electron/electron/blob/v45.0.0/docs/api/web-contents.md#event-will-frame-navigate) and [stable frame identity](https://github.com/electron/electron/blob/v45.0.0/docs/api/web-frame-main.md#frameframetreenodeid-readonly). No dependency version changed. The host uses a one-use private request grant to bridge supported request context into the protocol callback; `null` origin/referrer never supplies authorization.

Retained intermediate failures are local under the entry run root. `build-01.log` retains the initial TypeScript shape mismatch between request/header-hook details, corrected by accepting only their shared context fields. `engine-01.log` retains the obsolete data-URL expectation, replaced with exact transfer payload/hash/slot checks. `desktop-01`/`desktop-02` retain the initial inherited parent-CSP image denial; the parent and derived preview now permit the internal image scheme while the native frame guard remains restrictive. `desktop-04` retains an incorrect test expectation for a visible ok diagnosis; the established UI intentionally omits that panel. `desktop-05` and interrupted `desktop-06` retain the sandbox callback probe that could not settle: event/timer callbacks injected into the empty sandbox are not a reliable test controller. Synchronous native-image state and host-denial checks replace that probe, without enabling sandbox scripts.

The development-URL arm of `desktop-05` additionally retained an intermittent HTML-link observation after Return. Frame remounts now have explicit, unpredictable, main-registered mount identities, so a retiring frame cannot share the newer frame's identity. `desktop-final` then reached successful PNG/HTML/JPEG checks but timed out while closing immediately after Return. Late request callbacks could access a destroyed BrowserWindow getter; the host now caches the owner webContents identity and denies disposed/missing contexts without accessing destroyed native objects. Deterministic host controls cover retiring mounts, stale unmount cleanup, renderer loss and destroyed-window callbacks. These fixes address supported lifecycle hazards; the exact causal contribution to each earlier timing observation was not fully instrumented.

`desktop-07` (file) and `desktop-08` (Vite development URL) retain independent pre-final passes of full native PNG/JPEG links, byte/hash capture, foreign/missing caller denials, forged/revoked URL rejection, transfer failure/raw access, selection/retry races and exact historical export. `desktop-close-fix` records the post-mount/post-shutdown-fix file-shell pass and five host-boundary controls. Their tested revisions differ from the final acceptance identity below; they are supporting observations, not substitutes for it. The stopped runs receive no full-suite verdict. All original AR-T2-01 and helper-study artifacts remain in place.


The later `acceptance` development-URL arm retained the same post-Return HTML-link timing observation, despite the stronger mount guard; its host log shows no native navigation or denied request for that click. The test now waits for the new mount identity, srcdoc load and image decoding before its next native interaction, rather than treating a visible DOM node as completed remount readiness. `native-readiness` and the two `acceptance-final` reference arms pass with that control. The prior observation is retained with this instrumentation limit; it is not rewritten as a host denial or assigned a fully proven root cause.

`acceptance-final` passes eleven reference/session/PDF/host checks but fails the old workbench test's direct `version` IPC probe, which omitted the now-required load generation. That probe was a read-only evidence assertion, not a renderer selection: it now reads canonical verified versions through `Documents.version`, leaving the active renderer lease intact. The development workbench regression also uses the existing private-profile harness. `records-and-workbench` passes both protocol arms with response/denial logs retained before relaunch, and the corrected full mock-only workbench flow. It does not weaken host generation validation.

Final review identified an unintended restriction introduced during this implementation: invalidating the saved-content attempt at editor entry disabled the existing explicit-version export while a draft was open. Preview-resource revocation and saved-content availability are now kept separate. The editor retains the verified saved version for export, while save/discard initiates a fresh preview generation. Matching UI and document-session assertions cover this preservation correction; the final affected-flow run is recorded below.

## T1-F01 AR-Q4-C1-R1 feasibility evidence

The [local ticket](tickets.md#t1-f01-ar-q4-c1-r1-offline-feasibility-note) selects an offline source/API assessment. Entry HEAD is `a88f1bab6687a2234c689b4589b1035d8f4b6618` on `codex/cred-03`. The work used retained sources, API text, prior witnesses and static PE tables. It did not invoke ExifTool, a decoder, the Adobe reader or a qualification harness. The [coverage proof](../../design/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#ar-q4-c1-coverage-proof-for-the-agreed-study) defines the packet set and the distinct gates for closure and per-input completeness.

### Carrier inclusion and compatibility

- **Read dispatch reaches the carrier.** The retained 13.61 source binds PNG `caBX` to the JPEG 2000 box table (`PNG.pm:343-347,991-1028`). The JPEG APP11 `JP` branch assembles JUMBF and calls the same table (`ExifTool.pm:8359-8411`). Table selection recognizes `brob` with `xml `, `Exif` or `jumb` child types (`Jpeg2000.pm:485-510`). The subdirectory passes the enclosing `DataPt` and a child `DirStart`/`DirLen` (`1223-1250,1323-1333`). `ProcessBrotli` checks and reads at the parent-buffer origin and would also slice the compressed input to the end of that buffer (`1392-1420`). For the two retained compressed witnesses, the first four parent bytes are the box length. The function warns and returns success before decoding. The [prior raw and compressed controls](#bounded-read-only-witnesses-and-exact-retained-artifacts) distinguish dispatch from field reporting and supply the runtime observation.
- **The fixed writer and full readback have different reach.** In the selected XMP-only write, `PNG.pm:1017-1024,1652-1674` gates rewrite of this `JUMBF` subdirectory on its own edit-directory selection. The ordinary write copies the chunk when no replacement is prepared. The JPEG writer classifies a `JP` APP11 segment for group deletion and otherwise copies it without decoding the embedded box (`Writer.pl:6699-6703,6814-6831`). Neither group deletion nor JUMBF editing belongs to the fixed nine-property write. These are source observations for those branches, not a new write/preservation test or an exclusion from the combined packet set.
- **Disposition under the adopted boundary:** retain the recognized skipped carrier as an unresolved coverage obligation. The source changelog identifies Brotli metadata support for JXL, and the XML edit branch checks `IsJXL` (`Jpeg2000.pm:1251-1263,1603-1614`). The read-side recognition used by the witnesses has no such condition. The retained source/API basis establishes recognition and the skip, while format compatibility of this box in PNG/JPEG JUMBF remains unresolved. There is no source-backed unreachable-path exclusion for this invocation. The witnesses establish the branch, without settling normative validity or proving useful metadata loss in a writer run.

### Supplemental traversal and exact decoder

- **A bounded span is available at dispatch.** A supplemental path could capture the box occurrence, its parent mapping and the exact child payload bounded by `DirStart`/`DirLen`, then separate the four-byte type from the compressed bytes. Changing only the type lookup leaves the rest-of-parent slice wrong. For PNG, the mapping starts at the physical chunk data. For multipart JPEG, `DataPt` is assembled and the code labels `DataPos` as accurate for a single segment. Source correspondence needs an ordered APP11 fragment map, captured before assembly and header normalization, with ambiguous associations, duplicates and incomplete ranges accounted for. The current assembler groups by box type and sequence. That grouping alone does not establish distinct source occurrences for interleaved same-type boxes (`ExifTool.pm:8359-8409`). The two prior compressed witnesses cover only a single carrier each.
- **Decoding would add behavior to the study.** The current observer boundary permits recording a skipped carrier but preserves native dispatch, results and diagnostics. A separately selected supplemental traversal would need a distinct context with zero write calls. It would pass exact decoded bytes into the corresponding existing XMP, TIFF or JUMBF route and account for descendants, skips and final completion (`Jpeg2000.pm:1423-1447`). `ProcessJUMB` changes document numbers/group state (`777-797`), so extra traversal in the original context could alter baseline semantics. The code labels the compressed JUMBF branch untested. A corrected argument or a successful XML witness alone would leave the EXIF offset, recursive box and namespace/base64 paths, occurrence mapping and other transitive coverage to prove. There is a plausible boundary for a future amendment, but the retained artifacts do not yet support a complete method.
- **The exact DLL supplies no callable C decoder export.** Static inspection of the Windows x64 PE export table of the archive-matched `Brotli.xs.dll` found one function and one named export, `boot_IO__Compress__Brotli`. Its import table names `KERNEL32.dll`, `msvcrt.dll` and `perl532.dll`, with no delay-import directory. SHA-256 is `7a2d367a0ddea0c422647b85f51a43482679826d64b7616212d06bf3d3ef2b1e` in both retained 13.55 and 13.61 distributions. That identifies the binary boundary. It does not identify the linked Brotli version or the exact XS build source, and it provides no supported way to address an internal function.
- **The available Perl interface loses the decisive state.** The shipped `IO::Uncompress::Brotli` 0.004001 documentation offers one-shot bytes and streaming output blocks. The retained version-matched publisher XS shows `unbro` returning only decoded bytes, and `decompress` calling the streaming C API while returning only accumulated output. The latter drops the remaining-input count and the final result, so a call that finishes by needing more input and one that reaches terminal success lack a separate returned disposition (`Brotli.xs:26-41,60-89`). A successful fixture roundtrip or matching output cannot certify full consumption and completion for arbitrary carriers. The [earlier C API reference](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#next-step-recommendation-after-the-coverage-study) identifies the required interface shape: `BrotliDecoderDecompressStream` exposes remaining input and a terminal result. A future adapter would need to demonstrate terminal success, zero remaining bytes for the exact carrier span, and complete bounded output, with controls for truncation and unconsumed trailing bytes. The retained basis contains no separately identified source/binary pair for such an adapter, so it is a future candidate only.

### Candidate comparison and bounded disposition

The retained 13.55 copy has the same parent-origin lookup in `ProcessBrotli` and the same DLL. Its [known valid-input failures](#comparative-preservation-and-negative-control-observations) remain decisive. Retain 13.61's bounded positive valid-input results and its open AR-Q4 gate. A new production release would need an exact-artifact source/API comparison for the missing route, followed by candidate-specific qualification.

The retained Adobe v2025.03 source exposes `GetXMP`, `CanPutXMP` and `PutXMP` (`public/include/TXMPFiles.hpp:596-640,669-675`). The PNG smart handler finds an XMP `iTXt` packet, while the JPEG handler recognizes EXIF/main/extended XMP in APP1, Photoshop resources in APP13 and skips other segments in the inspected read loop. Copy-through of an unrecognized chunk/segment is distinct from raw inventory of its contents. These APIs merit a writer comparison, but they do not supply this skipped-carrier inventory. An SDK writer would also need a genuinely independent readback implementation, since the existing independent reader uses the same SDK. This is source evidence for the candidate boundary, not SDK writer execution or preservation qualification. [PNG handler](https://github.com/adobe/XMP-Toolkit-SDK/blob/581c41213ddcee1fbc72cbb532531102a6617a25/XMPFiles/source/FileHandlers/PNG_Handler.cpp) and [PNG chunk lookup](https://github.com/adobe/XMP-Toolkit-SDK/blob/581c41213ddcee1fbc72cbb532531102a6617a25/XMPFiles/source/FormatSupport/PNG_Support.cpp), [JPEG handler](https://github.com/adobe/XMP-Toolkit-SDK/blob/581c41213ddcee1fbc72cbb532531102a6617a25/XMPFiles/source/FileHandlers/JPEG_Handler.cpp).

**Feasibility disposition:** no justified qualification amendment on the retained basis. The unresolved compatibility and lack of an identified decoder that exposes consumption and terminal completion are enough to stop here. Even a suitable decoder would still need fragment/recursive source correspondence and observation-fidelity proof. The [recommendation](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#writer-reconsideration-after-t1-f01) explains the next decision and what would justify revisiting a supplemental 13.61 route. No writer or guard is qualified by this assessment.

**Identity and review scope:** rehashed the [retained 13.61 archives](#coverage-study-identities-and-guidance). The four cited ExifTool modules match both retained archive members and their inspected source/runtime copies. The PM and PE match the Windows archive. The retained publisher XS hash matches the [source audit](#source-audit-and-ar-q4-c1-r1). The cited Adobe header and three handler/support files match members of `adobe-v2025.03.zip`, SHA-256 `0ac5a4a29bb7bf1e3a93054b6e2bd2602258812659ead9e6000edbf2a1eb1598`, retained against the [original reader identity](#t1-artifact-build-and-environment-identities). This was repository/local-artifact inspection, without fresh publisher retrieval or native qualification.

**Documentation impact:** the ticket tracks this contribution, the discussion owns the writer recommendation, WORK_ITEMS routes the next decision, and CURRENT_STATE records the stop. The design, spec, phase acceptance, product pins and evidence from previous runs retain their scope. The note did not amend the observer boundary or the qualification matrix.

**Checks:** the repository document checker passed with 138 Markdown files, 6,862 local destinations and 503 verified bundled sources. Git and focused whitespace checks passed. Entry-relative hashes cover 881 maintained files and show changes only to the five records above. All earlier verification bytes remain an exact prefix. HEAD and the index stayed at entry.

## T1-F02 production ExifTool release evidence

The [local ticket](tickets.md#t1-f02-production-exiftool-release-source-check) was recorded before this source review. On 2026-11-09 (Asia/Singapore), entry HEAD was `a88f1bab6687a2234c689b4589b1035d8f4b6618` on `codex/cred-03`. Fresh publisher responses, exact-tag source reads, retained-archive comparisons and prior bounded observations supply the evidence.

### Release channel and exact artifacts

- **Production designation:** the publisher's live history designates **13.55**, dated 2026-04-07. MetaCPAN's release endpoint also reports `Image-ExifTool-13.55` as latest. The publisher's general download version is **13.61**, dated 2026-11-05 in the history. The history classifies versions beyond the designated production release as development releases. The publisher repository's pinned history at 13.61 agrees, and read-only remote refs identify HEAD at tag `13.61`. The GitHub Releases API latest endpoint returned 404. The publisher history and registry establish the channel. [Official history](https://exiftool.org/history.html), [pinned history](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/exiftool/exiftool/9b8664dfcf2c609d3cc8925d380e98ad2a379f9b/html/history.html), [version file](https://exiftool.org/ver.txt), [MetaCPAN release record](https://fastapi.metacpan.org/v1/release/Image-ExifTool).
- **13.55 identity:** remote tag `13.55` resolves to `de11d240cf7e521e939a0575a128ffe01b63be05`. The freshly fetched publisher checksum for `exiftool-13.55_64.zip` is `9fadaf5221dcb07a5d018e21c8529e257917d2fad1fdfc8e64855c4fb73293df` and matches the retained archive. Fresh commit-pinned `XMP.pm`, `PNG.pm`, `Jpeg2000.pm`, `ExifTool.pm` and `Writer.pl` match both the retained source ZIP and the Windows ZIP member bytes. For 13.61, the same five files from its own commit `9b8664dfcf2c609d3cc8925d380e98ad2a379f9b` match both retained 13.61 archives. [13.55 source tree](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/tree/de11d240cf7e521e939a0575a128ffe01b63be05), [publisher checksums](https://sourceforge.net/projects/exiftool/files/checksums-13.55.txt/download), [retained T1 artifact identities](#t1-artifact-build-and-environment-identities), [retained 13.61 artifacts](#comparative-artifacts-and-reproducibility-basis).

### Source comparison and qualification limits

| Check | Result on the exact production release |
| --- | --- |
| **AR-Q1: valid XMP CDATA and comment preservation** | In `13.55 XMP.pm:3806-3844`, the inner property-end scan can match a closing tag inside CDATA or a comment. Later CDATA value decoding cannot repair a property boundary already ended at the embedded token. The scan in `13.58 XMP.pm:3806-3855` recognizes those sections and advances to their terminators before resuming closing-tag matching. Checked `13.57` still has the old scan. The prior Windows comparison observed 13.55 changing unrelated valid values and 13.61 preserving them in the PNG and JPEG controls. [13.55 source](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/blob/de11d240cf7e521e939a0575a128ffe01b63be05/lib/Image/ExifTool/XMP.pm#L3806-L3844), [13.58 source](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/blob/38bdbace3037a89d2c332664fc60bddad118958a/lib/Image/ExifTool/XMP.pm#L3806-L3855), [observed results](#comparative-preservation-and-negative-control-observations). |
| **AR-Q2: PNG EXIF/JUMBF readback in either chunk order** | In `13.55 PNG.pm:1003-1028`, embedded directories have chunk-local offsets but enter processing with shared `PROCESSED` tracking. `ExifTool.pm:9039-9050` can treat an equal address as a repeated directory and skip it. `13.60 PNG.pm:1025-1028` localizes that tracking for each embedded PNG subdirectory, retaining the guard inside it. The checked 13.59 source lacks that localization and 13.61 retains it. Prior 13.55 readback omitted useful EXIF values for `caBX` first and warned in both orders even though the raw chunks survived. Both orders retained their expected fields in the 13.61 comparison. [Maintainer change and structural control](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/pull/453), [13.60 source](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/blob/980409314fddbd83d9092b5c160fafab7f67fd52/lib/Image/ExifTool/PNG.pm#L1025-L1028), [observed results](#comparative-preservation-and-negative-control-observations). |
| **AR-Q4-C1-R1: recognized Brotli carrier skipped before packet extraction** | 13.55 PNG `caBX` (`PNG.pm:343-347`) and JPEG APP11 `JP` assembly (`ExifTool.pm:8286-8334`) reach the shared box table. `Jpeg2000.pm:485-510,1223-1250` recognizes the child and supplies the parent `DataPt` with `DirStart` and `DirLen`. `ProcessBrotli` reads the type at parent offset zero and returns after warning for an unknown type, before the decoder (`1392-1420`). Even the later slice would use the rest of the parent buffer. The entire `Jpeg2000.pm` member is byte-identical in the 13.55 and 13.61 archives (SHA-256 `6d5647c841f5cb4c088d88ae9b0904bc62ab34d31f3f2b74819f5f846fb7b088`). Both ship identical Brotli PM and XS DLL bytes. The [T1-F01 static inspection](#supplemental-traversal-and-exact-decoder) supplies the DLL's bootstrap-only export and the interface's missing consumption/completion result. The [retained read witnesses](#bounded-read-only-witnesses-and-exact-retained-artifacts) were run on 13.61. The matching source and paths support the 13.55 inference. [Pinned box code](https://github.com/exiftool/exiftool/blob/de11d240cf7e521e939a0575a128ffe01b63be05/lib/Image/ExifTool/Jpeg2000.pm#L1392-L1420). |

**Disposition:** the identified production release leaves AR-Q1, AR-Q2 and AR-Q4-C1-R1 unresolved. The later development fixes have bounded runtime support for AR-Q1 and AR-Q2 and leave the recognized Brotli skip in place. The scoped PNG recursion guard change does not alter the box-buffer slicing. Neither the source comparison nor the prior positive valid-input matrix supplies complete packet inventory, normative carrier compatibility, strict pre/post rejection, or a new writer qualification. The [writer recommendation](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#writer-recommendation-after-t1-f02) owns the next selection proposal.

**Retained basis:** `.local/ar-t1-f02-20261109-2119a8e4/` contains 20 publisher/reference responses, `receipts.json` with URLs and SHA-256 hashes, and `refs.txt` with the read-only tag check. All response hashes were rechecked. Only source and public metadata were retrieved. The already-retained ZIPs were inspected in place, without launching a writer, reader or decoder. Earlier runtime results retain their original versions and scope.

**Documentation impact:** the ticket records completion, the discussion records the writer recommendation, WORK_ITEMS routes the next decision and CURRENT_STATE records the stop. The release policy, existing support, batch criteria, T3 gate and phase acceptance retain their prior scope.

**Checks:** the repository document checker passed with 138 Markdown files, 6,889 local destinations and 503 verified bundled sources. Git and focused whitespace checks passed.
