## T5 Windows-row and agent tool-path checks

2026-11-12, Asia/Singapore. The user [selected](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#windows-row-checks-and-agent-tool-path-check-selected) three offline checks on this Windows 10 Pro 22H2 build 19045 host with local NTFS. `LongPathsEnabled` was read as `1` and not changed. The basis is `4b22a02` plus the export correction below, using the development engine. No frozen build was made. No provider was contacted.

### Case, canonical and substitution collisions

One normal `edit_document` save created eight distinct occurrences on page 1 (printed `1`). Their filename labels form four pairs that share a portable comparison key. The first of each pair took the plain name and the second received the next suffix:

| Labels | Assigned basenames |
| --- | --- |
| `Map` / `MAP` (case only) | `Map-p1.png` / `MAP-p1 (2).png` |
| `Café plan` composed / decomposed `Cafe` + U+0301 | `Café plan-p1.png` / `Café plan-p1 (2).png`, both NFC |
| `Straße` / `STRASSE` (full case folding) | `Straße-p1.png` / `STRASSE-p1 (2).png` |
| `A:B` / `A：B` (substitution) | `A：B-p1.png` / `A：B-p1（2）.png` (full-width suffix for the full-width label) |

Version `783dd62f23b6435e9a80a0c06e05a435` reopened from a new store handle with the same eight assets. Its export, SHA-256 `f295959c7e6e0668669f801ed627eb654eef08d01efb6844f5bf795ba500cda1`, had 20 members matching the version's file hashes. Python `zipfile`, Windows `tar.exe` and PowerShell `Expand-Archive` each extracted all 20 members with exact bytes, and the on-disk asset names matched the logical spelling. The Markdown and HTML link targets equalled the asset set. Pinned Chrome for Testing 157.0.8103.38 decoded all eight inline images and followed each link to the exact expected file path.

**Control.** A hand-made ZIP holding `assets/Map-p1.png` and `assets/MAP-p1.png` extracted on NTFS to one file containing the second member's bytes. NTFS merges case-only names, and the app's portable key prevents allocating such a pair.

### Long whole-path behavior

Through the normal HTTP export route on the corrected engine:

| Destination | Result |
| --- | --- |
| 354-character path, ordinary ZIP name | Exported and verified. |
| 200-character ZIP filename (295-character path) | Exported and verified; only the ZIP remained in its folder. Before correction it failed as [AR-T5-01](#ar-t5-01--a-valid-long-zip-name-cannot-be-exported). |
| 304-character final component | HTTP 400 naming the destination and stating "One folder or file name has 304 characters; the limit is 255". Its folder stayed empty. Before correction this was [AR-T5-02](#ar-t5-02--an-unwritable-destination-returns-a-generic-server-error). |

Extracting the collision package under a 265-character folder (longest member path 347 characters): Python `zipfile` and `Expand-Archive` extracted all 20 members exactly. Windows `tar.exe` exited 1 with `could not chdir` to the target and wrote no files. Pinned Chrome refused the 278-character `preview.html` path with `net::ERR_FILE_NOT_FOUND`. No tool renamed or overwrote a member. These are visible external-tool limits, consistent with [R14](../../reviews/RUNTIME_FINDINGS.md#r14--deep-windows-file-url-assets-can-fail-to-display); short destinations remain the success case.

### Scripted agent tool-path flow

`tests/engine/documents/test_agent_asset_flow.py` drives the normal `Runtime`, Nexus Chat Completions transport and tool dispatch with a scripted model on an `httpx.MockTransport`. Every handle it sends is read from the tool results in its request payload. The seven model turns were `inspect_pdf`, two `render_page` calls, `edit_document`, `extract_image`, `edit_document`, `edit_document` and `report`.

1. Following the `render_page` guidance, the agent linked page 2's registered `asset_path` and saved schema 1 version `46671deeb6a54ab3990ff85b3b482387`.
2. It extracted the page 1 DCT figure, read the title, caption, figure label and printed `7` from the text, and saved schema 2 version `50fae359e96249d890972f5e48b0d665`. That save created `Harbour map-p7-Figure 1.jpeg` and adopted the page 2 hash path through `legacy_path` as `Field notes page-i2.png`, with printed state `unknown`.
3. It corrected the figure's description using the returned occurrence ID. Version `bb77ddeb39824b078f85023e5e5c98a8` renamed it to `Harbour chart-p7-Figure 1.jpeg` and returned the previous path.

The job completed with that version as its delivery. A new store handle reopened all three versions with the earlier ones unchanged. The export's members matched the version, and the Markdown linked exactly the two current assets. The structured record carried the source title and the known and unknown printed states. The seven requests reached only the mock transport. The run report is retained under ignored `.local/t5-winrow-be1b0fc6/`.

This shows that the tools give an agent every handle and fact the save needs. It does not show that a real model chooses this path.

### AR-T5-01 — A valid long ZIP name cannot be exported

P3. Export prepared its ZIP as `.{name}.{64-hex intent}.tmp` beside the destination. For a valid ZIP filename of about 186 or more characters, that sibling exceeds the 255-character component limit, so the export failed although the destination itself was valid. The failure is deterministic; actual user incidence is unknown. **Corrected:** the prepared file is now `.pdfmd-export-{16-hex intent}.tmp`, 34 characters for every destination. The 200-character case now passes.

### AR-T5-02 — An unwritable destination returns a generic server error

P3. An `OSError` from folder creation, preparation or publication reached the HTTP layer as status 500 `Internal Server Error`, without naming the destination. The desktop would show only a generic engine failure. **Corrected:** these errors become HTTP 400 with the destination path, the operating-system reason and, for an over-255 component, its length. Any prepared file is removed. A concurrent existing destination still returns the existing conflict and keeps prepared work.

### AR-T5-03 — `render_page` guidance steers delivery to schema 1

P2 for the enrichment outcome; incidence with a real model is unknown. The `render_page` description says "Use the registered asset_path for delivery". `edit_document` says to deliver a source image through an `asset_changes` create. An agent that follows `render_page` saves a schema 1 hash-path link. That link is portable, but it has no readable name or standalone provenance, so AR-8 is not met. The scripted flow reached schema 2 only through an explicit create. **Open:** the fix is a guidance change tied to the [schema 1 transition choice](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#t3b-review-and-open-integration-choices). See the [recommendation](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#next-step-recommendation-after-the-windows-row-checks).

### Checks and scoped result

The focused export regression failed with the original `documents.py` and passed with the correction. The full Python suite passed: 1,797 tests, exit code 0. Desktop Playwright tests and a frozen build were not run. Tested identities (SHA-256): `documents.py` `3956f2a8934730ca2f10b3c556cb930ab8a836a1cbec7e8db4877cc6ad53bc60`, `test_agent_asset_flow.py` `06f8e19aeca0e788ae4ff8ad8ea3702624b9be4de4a7a02237f73508a58a3aff`, `test_documents.py` `1777aaca38bb9143eecaf698689aa751d4b194ace1f28383e616dae77b6eaaf9`. Harness scripts, reports, logs, stores and archives are under ignored `.local/t5-winrow-be1b0fc6/`.

**Combined assessment.** The Windows row now has collision, long-path and agent-wiring evidence alongside the [integration run](#t5-local-integration-on-windows-10). T5 remains incomplete for four reasons: AR-T5-03 is open; a real model's evidence-to-save behavior is unexercised; the AR-8 and AR-12 publisher cases in the [criterion map](#t5-criterion-evidence-map) are open; and the desktop and frozen-engine chain ran on `971eccc`, before the export correction. Linux and macOS are [deferred](#destination-scope-after-the-windows-only-decision).

**Documentation impact.** The [criterion map](#t5-criterion-evidence-map), [T5 ticket](tickets.md#t5-local-execution), [runtime design](../../design/RUNTIME_AND_DELIVERY.md), [work register](../../WORK_ITEMS.md), [discussion](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#next-step-recommendation-after-the-windows-row-checks) and [resume route](../../CURRENT_STATE.md#focus-and-resume-route) were updated.

## T5 standalone checkpoint review

2026-11-12, Asia/Singapore. The review covers the [Windows-row and agent tool-path checkpoint](#t5-windows-row-and-agent-tool-path-checks) at `4b22a02` plus the working-tree export changes and untracked agent fixture identified above. The three source/test hashes match the earlier check record. The retained collision ZIP SHA-256 also matches. The two focused document test modules passed 19 cases, including the existing long-name HTTP control and the scripted save/adopt/correct flow. The synthetic reproductions below used isolated Windows task stores and destinations. No provider was called and no desktop or frozen run was repeated.

### AR-T5-04 — Export attempts can overwrite another file's bytes

P2, affecting [AR-6](spec.md#ar-6--exact-export-and-relocation-without-inference) and the [export preservation contract](../../design/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#export-and-existing-version-preservation). [Documents.export](../../../src/pdfmd/documents.py#L859) opens a deterministic sibling with ZIP mode `w`. Exclusive linking protects the final filename from replacement, but a writer with the same sibling can still modify its underlying bytes after publication.

- Pre-created an unrelated file containing a sentinel at the computed temporary pathname. The normal HTTP route returned 200 with a verified ZIP, and the sentinel file was gone. This check used a deliberately prepared name collision. Its incidence in ordinary folders is unknown.
- Started two exports of the same version to the same unused destination, each with its own store handle. Test hooks controlled when the calls reached ZIP open and hard-link publication. Both passed the initial destination check. Immediately after the first call linked a 1,501-byte verified preparation, the second opened that same sibling in `w` mode and the published path became zero bytes. The first returned an unverified receipt, the second raised the destination conflict, and the final ZIP contained no members. The hooks only ordered the real file operations. Same-destination concurrency frequency is unknown.
- The same staging mechanism and both failures reproduced with `documents.py` from `4b22a02`, using its old longer sibling name. The filename-length fix preserves an inherited defect. The scripts and logs are retained locally under ignored `.local/t5-review-8b7d41/`.

### AR-T5-03 — System instructions also steer delivery to schema 1

The [original observation](#ar-t5-03--render_page-guidance-steers-delivery-to-schema-1) stands. [The system instructions](../../../src/pdfmd/context.py#L30) also say to use registered `asset_path` values in Markdown images or embedded HTML. Normal [request preparation](../../../src/pdfmd/context.py#L126) sends those instructions alongside the tool definitions, so reconciling the tool descriptions alone leaves the conflicting direction in the same request. The retained mock report confirms the tool wording and the schema 1 then schema 2 save sequence. Actual model incidence remains unknown.

**Combined assessment.** The long-name and synthetic evidence-to-save checks still pass at their stated scope. The export counterexample blocks preservation acceptance and the guidance conflict remains open. The [criterion map](#t5-criterion-evidence-map) retains the publisher-source coverage gaps. The [follow-up discussion](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#t5-checkpoint-review-and-follow-up-proposal) proposes the next offline increment and its checks.

**Documentation impact.** Updated the [criterion map](#t5-criterion-evidence-map), [work selection](../../WORK_ITEMS.md#selection-now), [topic discussion](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#t5-checkpoint-review-and-follow-up-proposal) and [continuation](../../CURRENT_STATE.md#focus-and-resume-route). The [T5 ticket](tickets.md#r01cal-a03-delivery-01-t5) already calls for failure and replay verification. The existing design and phase conditions still apply. The repository documentation and Git whitespace checks passed.

All three native image links decoded and Return restored the selected document without another version read. ExifTool 13.55 recovered the matching records from three renamed standalone images. Copied ranges outside primary XMP and decoded pixels matched the source evidence. The task had zero model requests.

## Review of the AR-T5-03 and AR-T5-04 implementation

2026-11-13, Asia/Singapore. A standalone review of `2b8e2ef` and the records in `d8f820e`, requested by the user. The working tree was clean at `d8f820e`. No product code was changed. The document and runtime test groups passed (223 tests), and the repository documentation check reported zero errors. The export and guidance changes match the [selected proposal](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#t5-checkpoint-review-and-follow-up-proposal): each attempt creates its own preparation, verifies it and publishes by exclusive hard link. A same-intent retry or concurrent attempt settles against the published bytes, and the system instructions now agree with both evidence-tool descriptions. The review found three further items.

### AR-T5-05 — The context test group fails after the guidance change

P3 test regression; no product behavior is affected. `tests/engine/context` ran 430 tests and 2 failed: both cases of `test_usage_trial.py::test_veto_commits_once_and_survives_reopen` stop at `assert f["fits"]`. With `context.py` and `tools.py` restored from `4b22a02`, both cases pass. The fixture uses a fixed capacity of 11,500 and measured usage of 9,600, sized so the proxy request fits while the usage-anchored reservation triggers the veto. The longer instructions and tool descriptions push the proxy request over that capacity. The fixture's comment records the same rescaling after T3b enlarged the `edit_document` schema. The [same-revision record](#t5-offline-repair-and-same-revision-windows-verification) stopped its full engine run in the context tests, so this was not observed there. Rescale both values to restore the geometry, and run the whole engine suite once on the final revision.

### AR-T5-06 — A missing create still points the agent to a hash path

P3 guidance residual of [AR-T5-03](#ar-t5-03--system-instructions-also-steer-delivery-to-schema-1); actual model incidence is unknown. The likeliest agent mistake is to write `asset:new/<client_key>` in Markdown but omit the matching `asset_changes` create. On an empty base, `edit_document` then rejects the save with "Input asset handles must be resolved by the asset edit operation before publication. ... Use a registered assets/ path." That last sentence directs the agent back to a schema 1 hash-path link, against the reconciled instructions. A synthetic probe on an isolated store reproduced the message. The fix is to name the missing create, using its `client_key`, and the `facts.image_ref` source, and to mention `asset:id/<occurrence_id>` for an existing occurrence.

### AR-T5-07 — A retained export preparation is not identified

P3 usability. When an unrelated file appears at the destination during publication, the adopted [export contract](../../design/RUNTIME_AND_DELIVERY.md) keeps the verified preparation and reports a conflict. The preparation is a hidden file with a random name (`.pdfmd-export-*.tmp`), but the conflict message only says "Prepared work is retained". The user cannot find or use it, and each such conflict leaves another orphan in that folder. Either name the retained file in the message or discard it, since the version stays exactly exportable.

**Not filed.** The export's `OSError` handler also covers its receipt writes, so a local store failure would be reported as a destination error. `NamedTemporaryFile` creates owner-only (0600) files on POSIX, so a hard-linked export there would keep that mode. That matters only for [XPLAT-01](../../WORK_ITEMS.md#context-capability-and-delivery-work), and is recorded there.

**Assessment.** AR-T5-03 and AR-T5-04 are implemented as proposed, with the stated Windows evidence. AR-T5-05 must be fixed before the full suite can pass. AR-T5-06 and AR-T5-07 are small offline corrections. The [recommendation](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#next-step-recommendation-after-the-ar-t5-03-and-ar-t5-04-review) orders them with the remaining T5 scope. Logs are retained under ignored `.local/t5-review2-*/`.

## Standalone review of the T5 cleanup

2026-11-13, Asia/Singapore. Reviewed the pending AR-T5-05 to 07 changes against `d8f820e`. The four changed Python files matched the [cleanup run's hashes](#ar-t5-05-to-ar-t5-07-cleanup). This review reproduced the two veto cases, the canonical create guidance and the competing-file export case in four focused tests. All six ordinary-task guard cases also passed. The retained full-suite log contains 1,800 pass markers and exit 0. A fresh full-suite rerun was stopped after the focused checks and identity comparison. No provider, desktop or frozen-engine run was used for this review.

### AR-T5-08 — Handle guidance can prescribe an invalid retry

P3, [AR-2](spec.md#ar-2--rejection-and-candidate-preservation). The new [schema 1 rejection](../../../src/pdfmd/documents.py) selects advice by a case-insensitive prefix and suggests the whole tail as a `client_key`. In a fresh task, `![Map](asset:new/map.v1)` told the caller to create `map.v1`. Submitting that key failed `AssetCreate` validation. `asset:NEW/map` gave the same create advice even though the shared resolver requires the lowercase prefix. `<asset:new/map>` still failed after adding the advised create because that autolink cannot be rewritten to a relative URI. The probes preserved the candidate bytes and left head unchanged. The canonical inline link saved with its create. An unresolved `asset:new/next` on the resulting schema 2 base returned only “Resolve the input asset handle in this edit before publication.” Real-model incidence is unknown. Use the [shared reference interpretation](../../../src/pdfmd/asset_references.py) to distinguish unsupported syntax from a valid missing binding, and give the binding-specific repair in both save paths. Check a corrected retry for each class while preserving exact candidates and the existing base/head checks.

### AR-T5-09 — A competing directory discards the verified export preparation

P3, [export retention contract](../../design/RUNTIME_AND_DELIVERY.md#continuation-feedback-and-delivery-settlement). In a local Windows HTTP check, a hook created the destination after the attempt had prepared and verified its ZIP, just before the real `os.link` call. For a competing regular file, the endpoint returned 409, named both paths, preserved the competing bytes and retained a ZIP whose member hashes matched the selected version. For a competing directory at the `.zip` path, `os.link` also raised `FileExistsError`, but opening the directory to check for a matching export raised `PermissionError`. The outer handler returned 400 and cleanup deleted the preparation. The directory survived and the saved version can be exported to another path. This path predates the wording change. Treat a competing directory as a destination conflict and retain the preparation with the same path diagnostic. Verify both destination types and the identical-file retry on Windows.

### AR-T5-10 — The ordinary-task guards test misses the committed veto branch

P3 test coverage. `test_ordinary_task_guards_apply_without_consuming_trial_budget` still uses 11,500 capacity and 9,600 measured usage for `veto=True`. A probe with those exact inputs found that the proxy request failed to fit, preparation chose ordinary compaction and no veto was committed. All three guards passed under this setup, leaving cancellation, request-limit and active-time behavior after a committed veto untested by those cases. At the rescaled 12,000 and 10,100 inputs, the proxy fit and the request carried `veto_C` with a committed decision. This is the same fixture drift as AR-T5-05 and establishes no runtime failure. Rescale the guard cases and assert the disposition before testing each gate.

**Assessment.** The selected cleanup works for the two rescaled veto cases, a canonical missing create on the schema 1 path and a competing regular file. Recommend one bounded offline follow-up for [these findings](../../WORK_ITEMS.md#context-capability-and-delivery-work) before using a live run to assess unaided image delivery. The review did not change the [T5 acceptance scope](#t5-criterion-evidence-map) or select a repair or live run.

**Documentation impact.** The [document guide](../../guides/DOCUMENT_ENGINE.md#enriched-creation-path-t3b) now locates the specific guidance in the schema 1 path. The [runtime contract](../../design/RUNTIME_AND_DELIVERY.md#continuation-feedback-and-delivery-settlement) still states the retention requirement. The [execution guide](../../guides/EXECUTION_AND_DELIVERY.md#10-explicit-version-export-and-publication) and [README](../../../README.md) need no behavior update for this review. The [T5 ticket](tickets.md#t5-local-execution), [work register](../../WORK_ITEMS.md#context-capability-and-delivery-work) and [resume route](../../CURRENT_STATE.md#focus-and-resume-route) link to the review. Stage B and Stage D completion conditions still apply.

### AR-T5-11 — A corrupt competing ZIP can discard the verified preparation

P3, [export retention contract](../../design/RUNTIME_AND_DELIVERY.md#continuation-feedback-and-delivery-settlement). On Windows, an isolated local HTTP probe copied the already verified preparation to the destination just before the real `os.link` and corrupted one DEFLATE block. The competing ZIP opened and its member names matched the expected set. Reading the affected member raised `zlib.error`, outside the conflict handler's `OSError` catch and the verifier's `BadZipFile` catch. The endpoint returned HTTP 500 and cleanup deleted the verified preparation. The competing ZIP survived and a fresh destination exported successfully. Treat a decompression failure during destination comparison as a failed match. Check a corrupt competing ZIP with matching member names for 409, retained byte-exact preparation, and unchanged competing bytes. Keep the identical-ZIP retry control.

**Assessment.** The three selected repairs pass their reported scenarios. AR-T5-11 remains open in the [work register](../../WORK_ITEMS.md#context-capability-and-delivery-work). This review establishes no new desktop, frozen-engine or live adoption evidence.

**Documentation impact.** The [ticket](tickets.md#t5-follow-up-for-ar-t5-08-to-ar-t5-10) and [criterion map](#t5-criterion-evidence-map) distinguish offline repair evidence from remaining batch acceptance. The initial [destination table](#destination-disposition) now labels its historical scope and links the adopted Windows matrix and later evidence. The [document guide](../../guides/DOCUMENT_ENGINE.md#enriched-creation-path-t3b) accurately describes both save paths, and the [execution guide](../../guides/EXECUTION_AND_DELIVERY.md#10-explicit-version-export-and-publication) accurately describes the directory check. Repository documentation and Git whitespace checks passed. Live adoption and branch publication remain deferred.

No product code was changed by this review.

### AR-T5-12 — Other unreadable competing ZIPs still discard the verified preparation

P3, [export retention contract](../../design/RUNTIME_AND_DELIVERY.md#continuation-feedback-and-delivery-settlement); same boundary as [AR-T5-09](#ar-t5-09--a-competing-directory-discards-the-verified-export-preparation) and [AR-T5-11](#ar-t5-11--a-corrupt-competing-zip-can-discard-the-verified-preparation). The destination comparison in `Documents.export` still lists the exceptions it treats as a mismatch, and `zipfile` raises others for archives whose names match. A local Windows HTTP probe, built like the AR-T5-11 regression, wrote a copy of the verified preparation with one changed header field to the destination just before the real hard link:

| Competing ZIP | Exception from `archive.read` | Endpoint | Verified preparation | Competing bytes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Encryption flag set on the first member | `RuntimeError` | 500 | Deleted | Unchanged |
| Compression method 93 on the first member | `NotImplementedError` | 500 | Deleted | Unchanged |

The saved version stays exactly exportable to another path, so no saved work is lost. A ZIP with matching member names must appear in the publication window, so incidence is likely very low. By source inspection (not executed), the same exceptions would also turn the pre-check for an existing destination with a recorded intent into HTTP 500 instead of the existing-destination conflict; no preparation exists at that point.

**Remedy direction.** Each review has found one more exception type at this comparison. A failed read of the competitor has only one meaning there: the destination does not hold this version's export. Treat any exception from reading the competing destination as a mismatch in both the pre-check and the post-link comparison, and keep the existing `OSError` mapping for the attempt's own folder, preparation and publication errors. Extend the parameterized regression with the encrypted and unsupported-method cases, and keep the identical-ZIP retry control. No method choice remains.

**Checks and limits.** The probe is retained only in this session's scratch directory and is not part of the repository. No provider, desktop or frozen-engine run was used. The [criterion map](#t5-criterion-evidence-map) and the remaining T5 scope are unchanged.

**Documentation impact.** The [work register](../../WORK_ITEMS.md#context-capability-and-delivery-work) records AR-T5-12, the [topic discussion](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#next-step-recommendation-after-the-ar-t5-11-review) records the next-step recommendation, and the [resume route](../../CURRENT_STATE.md#focus-and-resume-route) points here. The execution guide and retention contract still describe the intended behavior.

## T5 live adoption run

2026-11-13, Asia/Singapore. The authorized 290-page News Writing PDF matched SHA-256 `9d23a3c4a519a1c04357a6f2e64093afd1c001034e8e4576561883c13b1cad4e`. The fresh task `a63462eca09248e0b2d1928e6fc856d2` used the exact [T5-L1 request and limits](tickets.md#t5-l1--bounded-live-adoption-run) and the existing subscription-only `cliproxy/gpt-6.1-sol` Chat Completions route at low effort. It retained A03's 180-second non-streaming deadline and two response repairs. Before Start, after the user reinstalled Nexus, the local catalog accepted the existing development credential and listed the selected model. The allowlisted local model/provider mapping matched A03. There was no inference in preflight and no change to the approved limits, fallback or optional mechanisms.

**Tested basis.** Development build from `aa14d35` with the product-code change identified in [T5-F12](#t5-offline-repair-for-ar-t5-12). Lockfile SHA-256 values were `8c3b138608dabfe33bd3ef9761f1bfce05cf71bd98a5181e93f5442d11386257` for `uv.lock` and `96512c26fe1efd3b0afa5b7a645f33fdbbec23434b80b2863eea929b4628a4ad` for `pnpm-lock.yaml`. The offline synthetic control exercised Cancel, relaunch, image decode and export through the desktop. The earlier A03 helper needed adaptation for the current preview-generation interface. A hidden-window screenshot timed out before Start and again after settlement. Structured monitoring and the ordinary app controls provided the run evidence. Retained scripts, diagnostics and identities are under ignored `.local/t5-l1-20261113-162057-24056/`, with the original durable store under `workspaces/t5-l1-24056/store`.

**Adoption: adopted after correction.** The first image edit (`:2:2:0`) used three `asset_changes` creates and matching authored handles with the three `render_page` refs. It was rejected with “A none fact needs evidence of absence and no alternatives” for unsupported caption and figure absence assertions. The next edit reused the retained blocks and removed those assertions, but was rejected with “Page 1: A none printed state uses printed 'none' with printed_basis evidence of absence.” The model then added the page evidence and independently saved schema 2 version `73335c3caf9e4554978353285bdfe095` on edit `:4:4:0`. A later edit classified the 287 outside-scope pages and saved `d0e54ea369724cd280ba62436441a452`, retaining the three enriched images.

**Settlement and usage.** One Start at 16:27:42, 12 physical app sends, 12 accepted native replies, and a thirteenth prepared request with zero attempts. The app stopped at `budget_exhausted` after 390.1 known active seconds and approximately 390 seconds of monitored wall time. The last four preview calls succeeded, and the model did not reach a terminal `report` before the ceiling. No operator Stop, Continue, replay or extra send occurred. Reported native usage was 346,276 input, 9,649 output and 355,925 total tokens. The 47,104 cached input and 395 reasoning output tokens are subsets. Subscription allocation, fees and any upstream internal attempts remain unknown.

**Reopen, package and image fidelity.** The task had no agent-selected delivery version. Review explicitly selected its latest partial draft `d0e54ea369724cd280ba62436441a452` after closing and relaunching the app. All three inline images decoded at 1241 × 1824 and Return restored the document. Desktop export created `outputs/t5-l1-24056/live.zip` with seven members, all byte-identical to the selected version, SHA-256 `8adb46baae11a33ed2a3179d20b1c44077202f1c937c2a179ab78373d51bf82e`. The readable names were `封面-i1.png`, `封底-i2.png` and `扉页-i3.png`. Each record correctly identified the source hash, physical page and evidence-backed printed-label state `none`. XMP insertion preserved the pre-enrichment PNG chunks, and the exported pixels matched fresh renders of the corresponding PDF pages. The model chose 150 DPI full-page PNG renders. The three embedded DCT streams remain in the retained PDF and were not extracted or included in the ZIP.

**Source-led review.** The structured map contains all 290 physical positions. It includes pages 1–3 and identifies pages 4–290 as outside scope with notes and unknown printed labels. The Markdown reproduces the cover, six back-cover endorsements, ISBN and price, and the title page. The draft and independent readback expose the two findings below. This establishes autonomous enrichment after correction and exact partial-draft delivery. Source fidelity and standalone source-title acceptance remain open.

### AR-T5-13 — Standalone images omit the visible source title

P2 for AR-8 on the exercised source. Pages 1 and 3 show the book title, and the model transcribed it in Markdown. Both saved versions and all three isolated exported images carry source title state `unknown`. ExifTool faithfully returns that state. The accepted edit omitted `source_update`, so the default unknown source facts remained. A copied image can be tied to the PDF by hash and page, but cannot identify the book by its visible title from the standalone record. This one run establishes the omission. It leaves the choice of guidance or validation, and the need for another live check, open.

### AR-T5-14 — Contributor name differs from the visible source

The delivered page images preserve the visible name, so a reader can recover it by checking the images. The text is unreliable for reuse of that attribution. The draft was preserved as produced.

### AR-T5-15 — Return can block images during preview frame startup

P2 for AR-5. Both `asset-references.spec.ts` cases on the tested revision (file and development URL) failed in `returnDocument` at image decode with `EncodingError: The source image cannot be decoded`. The file case reached the mixed-image link control. The development-URL case failed earlier. The retained `network.json` traces show the new, attached, named preview child with an empty frame URL on the blocked image requests. The same tests served other selected images with HTTP 200. The retained console evidence reports `ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT` for those blocked resources.

The network records support a frame-startup timing issue: `PreviewResources.frameAllowed` requires `about:srcdoc` before admitting the first request from a new named child, while Chromium can send the image request before that URL is visible. The observed failure affects in-app image display after Return. The separate byte-exact package and external viewer checks passed. The trace establishes the blocked request and affected user flow. A remedy must establish trusted frame identity throughout mount and Return while retaining the existing isolation and revocation checks. No change to the guard was made.

**Disposition.** T5-R is blocked at the desktop acceptance check. T5-V awaits a reviewed disposition and rerun of the required chain. The [criterion map](#t5-criterion-evidence-map) retains the successful Windows observations alongside the AR-5 failure. The work register and continuation route point to this finding. [Draft PR #1](https://github.com/hereiam510/pdf-to-md-agent-2/pull/1) carries the reviewable branch.

**Planning check, 2026-11-13.** The [T5-F15 plan](tickets.md#t5-f15--repair-preview-frame-startup) ties the first-image startup check to [AR-5](spec.md#ar-5--actual-desktop-reopen-and-link-use), preserves the failed run and precedes the existing Windows rerun and verdict. The [discussion](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#ar-t5-15-follow-up-plan-selected) records the user's agreement and execution boundary. The work register and continuation route link the selected work. Documentation link and Git whitespace checks passed. The existing product contract and phase acceptance still apply. No application checks were run for this planning update.

## T5 offline repair for AR-T5-15

2026-11-13, Asia/Singapore. Starting HEAD `e216925`, with the six local planning documents preserved. The retained baseline contains three denied image requests from attached, named preview children whose frame URL was empty. Electron 45.0.0 with Chromium 156.0.8078.48 exposed a unique direct child and the same frame-tree ID on its first image request across three probe remounts in each actual shell mode. Electron documents the lifetime of the [frame-tree identity](https://github.com/electron/electron/blob/v45.0.0/docs/api/web-frame-main.md#frameframetreenodeid-readonly) and the optional [request frame](https://github.com/electron/electron/blob/v45.0.0/docs/api/web-request.md#webrequestonbeforerequestfilter-listener).

The passing repair binds the initial empty child through trusted IPC before setting its first `srcdoc`. Earlier candidates using a preliminary `srcdoc` sometimes decoded images without painting the saved document. A failed layout trace had a nonzero iframe viewport and a fully loaded document with zero-size root and body. Explicit blank navigations also failed to progress reliably. Those attempts and the Windows import-path correction in the probe remain in the ignored `.local/t5-f15-20261113-171045-8898/` directory.

`pnpm build` passed, and the two real desktop cases plus six host tests passed in `focused-r06`. The file and development-URL traces are under `asset-references-IPPnKu/` and `asset-references-MPnGIW/`. Each Return bound the matching named child before the first image request, with matching frame-tree ID, successful decode and selected-version SHA-256 from that Return's actual responses. This included repeated native image views and the blocked external citation. The IPC trace recorded only unmount, reserve and bind for Return alongside the ordinary health/job polling. The negative controls denied missing, foreign, unrelated and retired frame contexts and rejected stale bind or cleanup while the replacement could still serve a new native image request. A host test supplies an empty request-frame URL after binding. The passing desktop traces did not happen to contain that timing edge. Empty sandbox, CSP, exact resource matching and the existing selected-version/export controls were retained. No provider calls were made.

**Documentation impact.** The [engineering guide](../../guides/DOCUMENT_ENGINE.md#shared-asset-reference-interpretation-and-selected-version-view) describes reserve, bind and first `srcdoc`. The [mount choice](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#ar-t5-15-execution-and-mount-choice) records the alternative and reason. AR-5, the batch tickets and the continuation route link the focused result. The sandbox contract, portable export and installed-product acceptance retain their existing scope. The focused evidence applies to the product and test tree committed with this section.

### AR-T5-16 — A shared schema 1 original can hide all selected preview images

P2 for AR-5 and AR-7. All six Windows `context-retry.spec.ts` cases, both `stencil-pins.spec.ts` cases and both `working-evidence.spec.ts` cases open saved schema 1 stencil deliveries made through local fixtures. The expected three `<img>` elements are absent. A comparison on copies of the `chat_completions history` store reproduced the same three “image unavailable” labels with the repaired renderer and the retained pre-repair desktop build. The repaired renderer bound its frame successfully and issued no image requests. The failed cases and separate diagnosis are retained in the rerun's `desktop-tests/` and `diagnostic/` directories.

The actual saved HTML contains all three images and the version passes registered-file integrity. The red and blue display assets declare distinct refs for one shared original path. The registered original, stored original, and bytes behind both refs match SHA-256 `77ce8ffa3e5e0bacf8b693a1edd26f22e7a4039aafca3c944e4020a977ecbc8a`. The two original blob records also have identical metadata. Schema 1 assessment compares the declared ref with the sole ref in the original inventory, so the red declaration fails that comparison. `Documents.selected` disables all bindings on the resulting `inventory_mismatch`. All ten fixtures returned zero image resources. This happens before the preview frame requests bytes. A direct export from the copied history store preserved all nine selected members byte for byte, and its separate reference assessment reported `incomplete`. The schema 2 shared-original rule has its own [verified correspondence](#ar-t4-02-correction). The schema 1 envelope requires a separate proof decision.

The failure blocks T5-R. Keep the product and test revision fixed and carry the finding to a separate decision before another rerun. A correction needs to distinguish genuine same-stream aliases from unsupported or foreign original refs without weakening registered-file integrity or changing immutable saved/exported bytes.

**Stop and documentation impact.** The [criterion map](#t5-criterion-evidence-map) links the passing Windows delivery evidence and the AR-5/AR-7 failure. The [finding disposition](../../WORK_ITEMS.md#context-capability-and-delivery-work), [technical question](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#shared-schema-1-original-verification-question), [engineering guide](../../guides/DOCUMENT_ENGINE.md#shared-asset-reference-interpretation-and-selected-version-view), README, [closing tickets](tickets.md#t5-closing-tickets) and [resume route](../../CURRENT_STATE.md#focus-and-resume-route) direct the next review to the affected boundary. The T5-R execution stops before T5-V and T5-P. The Windows-only scope, AR-12 amendment, T5-L1 partial-draft evidence, AR-T5-13 and AR-T5-14 remain applicable. Stage B and Stage D acceptance are still open. Repository documentation and Git whitespace checks passed.

### AR-T5-17 — Recovery diagnostics test compares an empty SQLite WAL as durable state

P3 for the desktop verification harness. In the Responses case of `recovery-diagnostics.spec.ts`, the test compared file hashes before and after opening saved diagnostic evidence. The first sample contained `catalog.sqlite-wal` with the SHA-256 of an empty file. The second sample lacked that sidecar. The main catalog, graph database and other captured files had identical hashes. The retained post-run catalog matches the hash in the failing assertion. The test stopped at its hash comparison before its later preview and export steps. The [SQLite WAL documentation](https://sqlite.org/wal.html#the_wal_file) explains the sidecar's connection lifecycle. The [existing recovery contract](../../guides/EXECUTION_AND_DELIVERY.md#read-only-observation-and-evidence-identity) already treats an empty WAL as containing no changed durable bytes.

The raw snapshot helper compares every file, including the empty journal, while the desktop continues its normal polling. That makes the suite sensitive to when the last connection closes. The evidence establishes a harness failure and does not show a change to durable task state. Preserve the raw failed suite and keep the product/test revision fixed during T5-R. A focused repeat can assess reproducibility. It does not supersede the failed full-suite result. Any correction must retain comparison of database files, nonempty WAL data and immutable outputs.

Three focused repeats of the failing Responses case passed on the same revision. A separate probe used Python's SQLite 3.53.1 and the same hash helper to reproduce removal of a zero-length WAL when a read connection closed. The main database hash and stored row were preserved. The first probe could not import the checkout's fixture package. The corrected run supplied the checkout on its import path. Retained evidence includes the failed full-suite trace, `wal-focused.log` and `wal-lifecycle-r02.log`.

**Stop and documentation impact.** T5-R remains open on the required desktop-suite result. T5-V and T5-P were not run. The [criterion map](#t5-criterion-evidence-map), [closing tickets](tickets.md#t5-closing-tickets), [work register](../../WORK_ITEMS.md#context-capability-and-delivery-work), README and [resume route](../../CURRENT_STATE.md#focus-and-resume-route) link to the new evidence. The installed-product and phase acceptance conditions still apply. Repository documentation and Git whitespace checks passed.

## T5 offline repair for AR-T5-17

2026-11-13, Asia/Singapore. The [user selected](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#recovery-snapshot-test-correction-selected) a test-only repair. The recovery fixture hashes the two main workspace databases, their nonempty WALs and all ordinary files. It omits empty WALs and the rebuildable shared-memory indexes of those two databases. If SQLite removes a WAL between enumeration and read, that sample records it as absent. A nonempty WAL captured in the other sample still differs. SQLite documents the [sidecar lifecycle](https://sqlite.org/wal.html#the_wal_file). This comparison is scoped to the fixture and uses the existing product [durable-state boundary](../../guides/EXECUTION_AND_DELIVERY.md#read-only-observation-and-evidence-identity).

All three focused Python checks passed. Real SQLite WAL fixtures for `catalog.sqlite` and `graph.sqlite` gave equal snapshots with and without empty WALs and their indexes. A committed change appeared in the nonempty WAL digest, then in the main database digest after checkpoint and close. The ordinary-file control detected modification and deletion and kept even empty files within a version directory. All four focused desktop recovery cases passed, including the Responses case that failed in the prior full suite. Both reopened-export audits checked the exact package and unchanged workspace bytes. Logs and fixtures are retained under ignored `.local/t5-ar17-20261113-193623-3206/`.

**Documentation impact.** The [closing tickets](tickets.md#t5-closing-tickets), [work register](../../WORK_ITEMS.md#context-capability-and-delivery-work), [selection](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#recovery-snapshot-test-correction-selected) and [resume route](../../CURRENT_STATE.md#focus-and-resume-route) record the new run. Windows `tar.exe` reproduced its deep-directory limit with exit 1 and no extracted files. All seven focused export controls passed, and the frozen HTTP route reconciled identical concurrent exports and an exact retry.

**Full suites.** The engine suite passed all 1,817 tests, exit 0, in 2,973.49 seconds. The desktop suite completed all 116 cases with 115 passes and one failure, exit 1. All four recovery-diagnostics cases passed, including the previous AR-T5-17 failure. Both preview modes and the ten cases previously affected by AR-T5-16 also passed. The distinct attribution-fixture failure below prevented a passing suite result. Product and test files stayed fixed throughout the run.

### AR-T5-18 — Attribution diagnostics test can race SQLite sidecar removal

P3 for the desktop verification harness. In the unreadable-head case of `attribution-reasons.spec.ts`, the `hash` helper listed `catalog.sqlite-shm` and then failed to open it with `FileNotFoundError`. The before-inspection sample on the reopened app failed before the helper could return a digest map. The Playwright trace and raw traceback remain in the Windows run. This test uses its own workspace hash helper in `tests/desktop/fixtures/attribution_reasons.py`, outside the AR-T5-17 recovery fixture. The error identifies a race in the harness. It does not establish a change to the durable workspace or a failure of the app's refusal message.

SQLite removes the rebuildable shared-memory index when the last connection closes. The [recovery engineering guide](../../guides/EXECUTION_AND_DELIVERY.md#read-only-observation-and-evidence-identity) explains the durable-byte boundary, and the [AR-T5-17 controls](#t5-offline-repair-for-ar-t5-17) demonstrate how to verify it. The same raw-snapshot pattern also exists in the task-diagnostics fixture. A separately selected correction should consider the shared comparison for desktop diagnostic fixtures that inspect a live workspace, keeping ordinary files and nonempty WAL data in scope. This ticket preserves the test revision and stops before the verdict and PR update.

**Stop and documentation impact.** The [criterion map](#t5-criterion-evidence-map), [closing tickets](tickets.md#t5-closing-tickets), [work register](../../WORK_ITEMS.md#context-capability-and-delivery-work), README and [resume route](../../CURRENT_STATE.md#focus-and-resume-route) link to the new evidence. T5-V and T5-P were not run. Stage B and Stage D acceptance conditions still apply. Repository documentation and Git whitespace checks passed.

### AR-T5-19 — Windows device aliases can block an enriched save

P2 for AR-9. On the accepted Windows host at the PR head, create a source page render from the local `rgb-jpeg.pdf` fixture and submit an enriched save through `POST /jobs/{job}/edit`. Use an inline or hyperlink `asset:new/img` target and set the create's filename description to `CON .study` or `NUL .notes`. The [generated-basename guard](../../../src/pdfmd/asset_naming.py#L115) tests the text before the first dot with the intervening ASCII space intact. The native Windows [DOS-device classifier](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/devnotes/rtlisdosdevicename_u) recognizes the generated `CON .study-i1.png` and `NUL .notes-i1.png` as devices. The normal edit returned `ASSET_INTEGRITY_FAILED`, retained the candidate and left head unchanged. The message reports an unreadable or changed asset and asks for a retry. The source image object was valid and the failure recurs for those generated names. The controls `CON.study` and `CON study` saved and exported successfully with `image-CON.study-i1.png` and `CON study-i1.png` respectively.

The existing [complete filename rule](../../design/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#readable-filenames-and-source-locators) requires repair of actual reserved aliases and preservation of ordinary text containing a device name. Before recommending ready-for-review status, correct that check and exercise the two aliases and both controls through normal save and exact export on Windows. The earlier passing collision fixture retains its bounded result.

### AR-T5-20 — The filename budget omits the device-name prefix

P3 for AR-9. Use the same save route and a filename description consisting of `CON.` and 164 ASCII `x` characters, with locator `i1` and PNG extension. The 168-byte description generates a 181-byte filename after the required `image-` prefix. The edit correctly returns `ASSET_NAME_BUDGET` and reports 173 remaining description bytes. The 167-byte version with one fewer `x` saves and exports at exactly 180 bytes. The [budget calculation](../../../src/pdfmd/asset_naming.py#L120) excludes the six-byte prefix from both the fixed-component count and the remaining-description calculation. That feedback can send the agent through another rejected edit with a description that fits the reported budget. Correct it with the related naming guard and recheck the exact boundary. The filename ceiling held in both cases.

**Readiness.** AR-T5-19 blocks recommending that this head leave draft. AR-T5-20 belongs in the same naming correction. The review introduces no new product requirement. The [AR-9 acceptance condition](spec.md#ar-9--portable-spelling-exact-180-byte-limit-and-semantic-shortening) already covers reserved names and exact filename budgets. Existing T5 results and phase conditions retain their original scope. Review probes and outputs are under ignored `.local/pr1-review-20261113-222230-6469/`. The first HTTP probe used an extra `edit_mode` field and failed request validation. The corrected probe used the supported edit shape. Repository documentation and Git whitespace checks passed. This standalone review recorded the findings locally and left the PR head, code and tests unchanged.

GitHub reported the PR as an open draft that merges cleanly into `master`, with no check runs. No product code was changed by this review.

**Ticket coverage.** T5-P pushed the branch and opened the draft PR. T5-F12, T5-L1, T5-G1 and the F15–F20 follow-ups each have a selection record, a verification section and a commit. T5-R passed on `8ccf461` after five fixed-revision attempts: 1,820 engine tests and 116 desktop cases passed, along with the builds, the delivery chain and the Windows-row controls. T5-V and the verdict update record criterion-level results. The batch is reported as complete with open evidence, not as accepted.

**Code inspected.** The review read the product-code diffs for each repair:

- **AR-T5-12 (export).** `holds_this_export` treats any read failure of a competing destination as a mismatch in both comparisons. Verification of the attempt's own preparation and published file stays strict.
- **T5-G1 (guidance).** The system instructions and the `edit_document` description now name `source_update` and evidence of absence for the first enriched save. The schema and validation are unchanged.
- **AR-T5-15 (preview startup).** The renderer reserves the mount, binds the inert child through IPC and assigns `srcdoc` only after the bind. The host admits requests only from the bound frame-tree ID with the reserved name.
- **AR-T5-16 (shared schema 1 originals).** The alias check applies only when the version already holds the verified original. Both task registrations must match its digest, kind and complete provenance.
- **AR-T5-19 and AR-T5-20 (naming).** The device-name guard ignores trailing ASCII spaces before the first dot and covers `CONIN$` and `CONOUT$`. The reported budget includes the prefix.

Each change matches its selected method, and the retained evidence covers its stated cases.

**Fresh checks.** All tests in `tests/engine/documents` passed at `c7fe4c3`. The repository documentation check reported zero errors. The review did not rerun the desktop suite, the frozen engine or any provider call.

**Noted, not filed.**

- **Preview remount.** `SavedPreview` does not reset its `bound` state if the generation changed without a remount. Today the component remounts on every reload, because the `previewKey` and the loading state unmount it, so no failing path exists. A future change that reloads a preview in place should reset the binding.
- **Competing ZIP reads.** The export comparison decompresses each competing member before comparing it. Checking `ZipInfo.file_size` first would avoid reading a mismatched large member. This falls under [R06](../../WORK_ITEMS.md#runtime-and-engineering-inventory) resource safeguards; no failure has been demonstrated.

**Readiness.** The Windows delivery chain passes, and no new defect was found. The remaining gaps are evidence questions under AR-8 and AR-9:

- whether a real model now carries the visible title into its first enriched save ([AR-T5-13](#ar-t5-13--standalone-images-omit-the-visible-source-title));
- transcription fidelity ([AR-T5-14](#ar-t5-14--contributor-name-differs-from-the-visible-source));
- a real unresolved printed label;
- source-led shortening.

T5-L1 also ran out of its 12-send allowance before a terminal report; two of those sends went to rejected saves. The [recommendation](../../discussions/ASSET_REFERENCES.md#next-step-recommendation-after-the-t5-closing-review) sets out the closing choice.

## T5 live re-run after the guidance clarification

**Tested basis.** On 2026-11-13 (Asia/Singapore), T5-L2 used the [exact T5-L1 request](tickets.md#t5-l1--bounded-live-adoption-run) in fresh task `50b2d928e8d844ada37f61add9272579`. The local PDF and immutable import matched at 290 pages and SHA-256 `9d23a3c4a519a1c04357a6f2e64093afd1c001034e8e4576561883c13b1cad4e`. The first three pages each contain one full-page DCT JPEG: RGB object 2137, RGB object 3 and gray object 7. The cover stream matched SHA-256 `228660d981f8af0dc7415d84f8b4b1cb06b052a416e7129f2e9cdeb4f715088b`. The development build used branch head `ee25ecf34f54b7941a1123d2ae5bebcd06585999`. `git diff 8ccf461 HEAD -- src` was empty. Lockfile SHA-256 values were `8c3b138608dabfe33bd3ef9761f1bfce05cf71bd98a5181e93f5442d11386257` for `uv.lock` and `96512c26fe1efd3b0afa5b7a645f33fdbbec23434b80b2863eea929b4628a4ad` for `pnpm-lock.yaml`. The executed development main bundle SHA-256 was `3b721183795991e2233f7811a7ffa5791be5d2e994a10d3fb3b0dfd43acd94c6`. The frozen system and tool descriptions contained the T5-G1 guidance. Only the PDF and the exact task request were supplied to the converter.

**Readiness and limits.** The allowlisted local Nexus mapping matched the user-confirmed subscription-only route. The local model catalog accepted the development credential and listed `cliproxy/gpt-6.1-sol`. The app loaded the ignored credential through `tools/dev/start.ps1`. The isolated synthetic control exercised Cancel, close and relaunch, image decode and desktop export at zero inference. The live launch confirmed the enabled Start, readable activity and a fresh store with no requests, attempts, versions or delivery. The live settings matched T5-L1: Chat Completions at low effort, vision on, `bpe_proxy_v1`, context/output/image allowances of 131,072/8,192/4,096, the 180-second non-streaming deadline and two response repairs. The ceilings were 12 physical sends, 1,200 known active seconds and a 1,500-second wall-time Stop, with one attempt per request. Replay, trusted scripts, observer and usage anchoring were off.

**AR-T5-13 resolved in this run.** The first image edit, `:2:2:0`, used three `asset_changes` creates with `facts.image_ref` from the three `render_page` results and saved schema 2 version `903e33b42eaa4054aed88b1cd0f3ee39`. Both saved versions record the visible title and subtitle from pages 1 and 3 as the known source title. ExifTool returned that title and its evidence from all three isolated, renamed images. The first enriched save supplied this `source_update` (formatted for readability):


**Rejected saves and absence evidence.** Zero rejected `edit_document` calls, so there are no rejection messages to quote. The first save set the printed label, caption and figure label to `none` for all three pages. Each printed state had `printed_basis`, and each absent caption and figure label had its own `basis`, all tied to the corresponding full-page render. Both saves succeeded. A separate `read_document` call ranged over the `/content/pages` object and received the expected range-type rejection. The model read the object without a range on the next send. This was a read error under the documented string/array restriction.

**Ending and usage.** The sole Start was at 23:50:16.036 SGT, with the Stop threshold at 00:15:16.036 SGT on 2026-11-14. On send 10 the accepted terminal `report` operation `:9:9:0` returned `complete` and selected `277b6cc78e874d0bbeab6fe472c766fb`. The task settled as `completed` with `stop_reason=agent_report` after 350.219 known active seconds and 351.199 seconds of monitored wall time. Monitoring began before Start, confirmed the enabled Stop control and recorded 354 job/activity samples including preflight and settlement, with a maximum gap of 1.018 seconds. All 10 sends were normal requests with one accepted attempt each and no unsettled attempt. No compaction or response-repair sends, operator Stop, Continue, replay, extension or coaching occurred. The native replies reported **311,947 input, 9,879 output and 321,826 total tokens**. Cached input (41,216) and reasoning output (133) are subsets. Usage was present for all 10 sends. Subscription allocation, fees and upstream internal attempts remain unknown.

**Selected version, images and standalone readback.** After closing the development app and relaunching, the reviewer selected the delivery version explicitly. All three inline images decoded at 1323 × 1946 before and after Return, and the preview text matched across the remount. The desktop exported `outputs/t5-l2-234748-b5531/live.zip`, SHA-256 `0348b67c1e0cc78118469cd54d75425a2cc8651daac515382990e12348e69220`. Its seven members matched the saved inventory and member bytes. Requests, attempts and active time stayed at the settled values through reopen and export. ExifTool 13.55 independently recovered the known title, source SHA-256, physical page and evidence-backed printed state from each image after copying it alone into an empty folder and renaming it. The readable XMP description and structured record agreed.

| Physical page | Exported basename | Printed state |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `前封面整体设计-i1.png` | `none` |
| 2 | `封底推荐语与条形码-i2.png` | `none` |
| 3 | `题名页与文具摄影设计-i3.png` | `none` |

The model chose three full-page 160 DPI PNG renders. Their copied PNG ranges matched the pre-enrichment PNGs and their pixels matched fresh renders of the PDF pages. No DCT originals were retained in the version or export. The three embedded streams remain in the immutable source PDF.

**Source-led review.** The saved Markdown preserves the covers and title page in physical order, with searchable titles, author and publisher, the cover's six recommenders, all six back-cover endorsements, ISBN, price and barcode digits. The added scope notes and image descriptions are identified as editorial or agent-authored. Review found no substantive discrepancy in the scoped transcription. For [AR-T5-14](#ar-t5-14--contributor-name-differs-from-the-visible-source), the Markdown correctly spells `李炳库` on pages 1 and 2. This is an observation on this run for Stage B. The saved map contains every physical position. Pages 1–3 are included, and pages 4–290 are outside scope with notes and unknown printed labels.

**Evidence and records.** The ignored `.local/t5-l2-234748-b5531/` retains preparation, monitoring, settlements, readback and source review. `workspaces/t5-l2-234748-b5531/store` retains the frozen payloads, native responses, candidates, receipts, versions and usage. No new product defect was observed. The AR-8 criterion row and AR-T5-13 work-register row link to this evidence. The ticket table links the completed run, and CURRENT_STATE routes to the separate verdict ticket. The repository documentation and Git whitespace checks passed.
